BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
with linear growth. These results cannot be directly applied to
E^{a}(u,\cdot)
and
E^{j}(u,\cdot)
, since functionals in
\mathfrak{E}
are not bounded from below by the total variation measure
|Du|
. This difficulty is treated by restricting our attention first to functions
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A,{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, and by considering the perturbed functionals
E^{\delta}(u,A):=E(u,A)+\delta|Du|(A)
. The absolutely continuous part and the jump part of
E^{\delta}
can then be represented by means of an integral thanks to
[
9
]
. We then show that it is possible to pass to the limit as
\delta\to 0^{+}
and to recover an integral representation of
E^{a}(u,\cdot)
and of
E^{j}(u,\cdot)
for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.

The following result then allows us to obtain the result for a general
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
, let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, let
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, and let
(R_{m})_{m}
be a sequence with
R_{m}>0
and
R_{m}\to+\infty
. Then

(5.1)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)\Big)=E(u,B),

(5.2)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{a}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)\Big)=E^{a}(u,B),

(5.3)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{s}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)\Big)=E^{s}(u,B),

(5.4)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{j}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)\Big)=E^{j}(u,B),

(5.5)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{c}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)\Big)=E^{c}(u,B),

for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

Proof.

For every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
we set

\mu_{m}(B):=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,B)\quad\text{ for every}B\in\mathcal{B}(A)

and note that this defines a finite Radon measure, being
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.

Let
U\in\mathcal{A}(A)
. Thanks to property (g) of Definition
3.8
, we have that

\mu_{m}(U)\leq E(u,U)+c_{4}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(U_{u,0}^{R_{m}})+\frac{C}{m}\left(E(u,U)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(U)\right).

Taking the limsup for
m\to+\infty
in the previous inequality, we get

\limsup_{m\to+\infty}\mu_{m}(U)\leq E(u,U).

For every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists
i(m)\in\{1,...,m\}
such that

E(\psi^{i(m)}_{R_{m}}\circ u,U)\leq\mu_{m}(U).

Since
\psi^{i(m)}_{R_{m}}\circ u\to u
in
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
when
m\to+\infty
and
E(\cdot,U)
is lower semicontinuous with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, we have

E(u,U)\leq\liminf_{m\to+\infty}E(\psi^{i(m)}_{R_{m}}\circ u,U)\leq\limsup_{m\to+\infty}\mu_{m}(U)\leq E(u,U),

so that (
5.1
) holds for every
U\in\mathcal{A}(A)
. From
[
21
, Lemma 4.4]
we then deduce that (
5.1
) holds for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

To show that (
5.2
) holds true, consider a set
N\in\mathcal{B}(A)
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N)=0
, such that for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
we have
E^{a}(u,B)=E(u,B\setminus N)
and
\mu^{a}_{m}(B)=\mu_{m}(B\setminus N)
for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, where
\mu^{a}
is the absolutely continuous part of
\mu
with respect to
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
.
By (
5.1
) we have that

\lim_{m\to+\infty}\mu_{m}^{a}(B)=\lim_{m\to+\infty}\mu_{m}(B\setminus N)=E(u,B\setminus N)=E^{a}(u,B),

which proves
(
5.2
)
. Taking the difference of (
5.1
) and of (
5.2
) we obtain (
5.3
).

Finally,
equalities (
5.4
) and (
5.5
) can be obtained arguing as in
[
21
, Proposition 4.3]
, replacing Theorem 2.2(d) with our Proposition
2.4
(d).
∎

The following result shows that the singular part
E^{s}
satisfies a simplified version of property (g) of Definition
3.8
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
,
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
,
R>0
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
, and
w\in W^{1,1}_{\rm loc}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Then

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{s}(w+\psi^{i}_{R}\circ(u-w),B)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{m}\Big)E^{s}(u,B),

where
C=\max\{9c_{3}k/c_{1},2c_{3}k^{1/2},c_{4}\}
.

Proof.

Let
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
be a Borel set, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N)=0
, such that
E^{s}(u,B)=E(u,B\cap N)
and
E^{s}(w+\psi^{i}_{R}\circ(u-w),B)=E(w+\psi^{i}_{R}\circ(u-w),B\cap N)
for every
i\in\{1,...m\}
, and for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
. Since (
3.5
) holds for every Borel set
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{s}(w+\psi^{i}_{R}\circ(u-w),B)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E(w+\psi^{i}_{R}\circ(u-w),B\cap N)

\displaystyle\quad\leq E(u,B\cap N)+\int_{B^{R}_{u,w}\cap N}(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\nabla w|+c_{4})\,dx+\frac{C}{m}\left(E(u,B\cap N)+\int_{B\cap N}(|\nabla w|+1)\,dx\right)

\displaystyle\quad=E(u,B\cap N)+\frac{C}{m}E(u,B\cap N)=\Big(1+\frac{C}{m}\Big)E^{s}(u,B),

which proves the claim.
∎

We now introduce the perturbed functionals
E_{\delta}
which will play a fundamental role in the proof of our representation result for functionals in
\mathfrak{E}
.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
and let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
be given. For every
\delta>0
the functional
E_{\delta}\colon BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\times\mathcal{B}(A)\to[0,+\infty)
is defined for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
as

(5.6)

E_{\delta}(u,B):=E(u,B)+\delta|Du|(B).

Given
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, the measures
E_{\delta}^{a}(u,\cdot)
,
E^{s}_{\delta}(u,\cdot)
,
E^{j}_{\delta}(u,\cdot)
, and
E_{\delta}^{c}(u,\cdot)
are defined as in Definition
5.1
with
E
replaced by
E_{\delta}
.

Remark 5.5
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, and
\delta>0
. Thanks to (c1
′
) and (c2
′
) of Remark
3.10
we have that for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
it holds

\delta|Du|(A)-c_{2}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)\leq E_{\delta}(u,A)\leq(c_{3}+\delta)|Du|(A)+c_{4}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A).

Definition 5.6
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
and let
E\colon BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\times\mathcal{B}(A)\to[0,+\infty]
. For every
U\in\mathcal{A}(A)
with Lipschitz boundary and
w\in BV(U;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, we set

(5.7)

m^{E}(w,U):=\inf\{E(u,U):\,\,u\in BV(U;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}),\,\,\textup{tr}_{U}u=\textup{tr}_{U}w\},

where
\text{tr}_{U}\colon BV(U;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\to L^{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}}(\partial U;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
is the trace operator.
Given
t>0
, we set

(5.8)

m^{E}_{t}(w,U):=\inf\{E(u,U):\,\,u\in BV(U;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}),\,\,\textup{tr}_{U}u=\textup{tr}_{U}w,\,\,\|u-w\|_{L^{\infty}(U;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq t\}.

We now introduce some functions which will play a crucial role in the integral representation of the bulk part
E^{a}
and the surface part
E^{j}
of a functional
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
. We recall that the cubes
Q(x,\rho)
and
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)
are defined in (f) of Section 2, while the functions
\ell_{\xi}
and
u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
are defined in (h) of Section 2.

Definition 5.7
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
and
\delta>0
. We define the functions
f,f_{\delta}\colon{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
and
g,g_{\delta}\colon{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
as

(5.9)

\displaystyle\displaystyle f(x,\xi):=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}\quad\text{for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\text{ and }\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d},

(5.10)

\displaystyle\displaystyle f_{\delta}(x,\xi):=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}\quad\text{for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\text{ and }\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d},

(5.11)

\displaystyle\displaystyle g(x,\zeta,\nu):=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\,\,\,\,\text{ for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},\,\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k},\text{ and }\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},

(5.12)

\displaystyle\displaystyle g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu):=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\,\,\,\,\text{ for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},\,\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k},\text{ and }\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}.

Remark 5.8
.

It is immediate to check that the functions
\delta\mapsto f_{\delta}(x,\xi)
and
\delta\mapsto g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu)
are non-decreasing and that

f(x,\xi)\leq f_{\delta}(x,\xi)\,\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\,g(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu)

for every
\delta>0
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

When
E_{\rm sc}
, we shall see that the functions
f_{\delta}
and
g_{\delta}
will be used in the integral representation of
E^{a}_{\delta}
and
E_{\delta}^{j}
thanks to the results of
[
9
]
. This will lead to an integral representation of
E^{a}
and
E^{j}
by means of the functions (see the proof of Theorem
5.16
)

(5.13)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{f}(x,\xi):=\inf_{\delta>0}f_{\delta}(x,\xi)\quad\text{for every $x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},$ $\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}$,}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{g}(x,\zeta,\nu):=\inf_{\delta>0}g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu)\quad\text{for every $x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},$ $\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}$, $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}$.}

For the applications to homogenisation it is important to prove that
\hat{f}=f
and
\hat{g}=g
, so that by (
5.9
) and (
5.11
) the integrands used in the bulk and surface part can be obtained by solving some auxiliary minum problems on small cubes.

The proof of the equality
\hat{f}=f
is not direct and requires a lot of technical tools, one of which being the following truncation lemma. For future use we prove the result also for the rectangles
Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
, defined in (f) of Section 2. Given
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
and
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we set

(5.14)

c_{\xi,m}:=(\sigma^{m}+1)d^{1/2}\Big(|\xi|+\frac{c_{2}+c_{4}+1}{c_{1}}\Big)

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
. Assume that there exists a function
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{F}
such that

(5.15)

E^{a}(u,A)=\int_{A}\hat{f}(x,\nabla u)\,dx,

for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
and
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.
Then there exists a set
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N)=0
, satisfying the following property: for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
\lambda\geq 1
there exists
\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m,\xi}(x)>0
such that for every
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m,\xi}(x))
and
u\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
,
there exists
v\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}v=\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
and
\|v-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq c_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho
, satisfying the inequality

(5.16)

\displaystyle E(v,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{m}\Big)E(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{C}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

where
C
is a constant depending only on the structural constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5}
, and
k
. Moreover, if
f
is continuous on
{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, then
N=\varnothing
. Finally, if
\hat{f}
is independent of
x
, then
\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m,\xi}(x)=+\infty
.

Proof.

Let us fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Arguing as in
[
21
, Lemma 4.16]
, one can construct a Borel function
\omega_{m}:{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
such that

(5.17)

f(x,\omega_{m}(x))\leq f(x,\xi)+\frac{1}{m}

for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
.
Note that (f2
′
), (f3), and (
5.17
) imply that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
one has

(5.18)

|\omega_{m}(x)|\leq\frac{c_{2}+c_{4}+1}{c_{1}}.

Since
\omega_{m}\in L^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d})
, by the Lebesgue Differentiation Theorem there exists
N_{m}\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N_{m})=0
, such that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N_{m}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
\lambda\geq 1
there exists
\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m,\xi}(x)>0
such that for every
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m,\xi}(x))
we have

\int_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\omega_{m}(x)-\omega_{m}(y)|\,dy\leq\frac{1}{m}\rho^{d}\lambda^{d-1}.

We set
N:=\bigcup_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}N_{m}
and note that
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N)=0
.
Using (f5) we get that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m,\xi}(x))
we have

(5.19)

\int_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\hat{f}(y,\omega_{m}(x))-\hat{f}(y,\omega_{m}(y))|\,dy\leq\frac{c_{5}}{m}\rho^{d}\lambda^{d-1}.

For every
y\in Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
and
i\in\{1,...,m\}
we set

\displaystyle\displaystyle w(y):=\omega_{m}(x)(y-x)+\ell_{\xi}(x),

(5.20)

\displaystyle\displaystyle R:=d^{1/2}\Big(|\xi|+\frac{c_{2}+c_{4}+1}{c_{1}}\Big)\lambda\rho,

\displaystyle\displaystyle v^{i}(y):=w(y)+\psi_{R}^{i}(u(y)-w(y)).

Note that with this choice of
R
we have
|\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}(u-w)|\leq R

{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}
-a.e. on
\partial Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
.
Recalling (
3.4
), we obtain
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}v^{i}=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
and

\|v^{i}-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq(\sigma^{m}+1)R=c_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho.

We claim that there exists
i\in\{1,...,m\}
such that (
5.16
) holds with
v=v^{i}.
To this aim, for every
i\in\{1,...,m\}
we consider the following partition of
Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
:

\displaystyle Q^{i}_{\rm in}:=\{y\in Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\colon|u(y)-w(y)|\leq\sigma^{i-1}R\},

\displaystyle Q^{i}:=\{y\in Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\colon\sigma^{i-1}R<|u(y)-w(y)|<\sigma^{i}R\},

\displaystyle Q^{i}_{\rm out}:=\{y\in Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\colon|u(y)-w(y)|\geq\sigma^{i}R\}.

Using (b) of Definition
3.8
we may write

E(v^{i},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))=E(v^{i},Q^{i}_{\rm in})+E(v^{i},Q^{i})+E(v^{i},Q^{i}_{\rm out}).

Recalling (
3.4
) and (
5.20
), from (
5.15
) we obtain

E^{a}(v^{i},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))=\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm in}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla u(y))\,dy+\int_{Q^{i}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla v^{i}(y))\,dy+\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm out}}\hat{f}(y,\omega_{m}(x))\,dy.

Taking advantage of (
5.18
)-(
5.20
), by (f2
′
) and (f3) we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{i}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla v^{i}(y))\,dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{3}k^{1/2}\int_{Q^{i}}|\omega_{m}(x)+\nabla\psi^{i}_{R}(u(y)-w(y))(\nabla u(y)-\omega_{m}(x))|\,dy+c_{4}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(Q^{i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{3}k^{1/2}\int_{Q^{i}}|\nabla u(y)|\,dy+(2c_{3}k^{1/2}|\omega_{m}(x)|+c_{4}){\mathcal{L}}^{d}(Q^{i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{3}k^{1/2}}{c_{1}}\int_{Q^{i}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla u(y))\,dy+\Big(2c_{3}k^{1/2}\frac{c_{2}+c_{4}+1}{c_{1}}+c_{2}+c_{4}\Big){\mathcal{L}}^{d}(Q^{i}).

By (
5.17
) and (
5.19
) we have

\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm out}}\hat{f}(y,\omega_{m}(x))\,dy\leq\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm out}}\hat{f}(y,\omega(y))\,dy+\frac{c_{5}}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}\leq\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm out}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla u(y))\,dy+\frac{c_{5}+1}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

From the previous inequalities, we get

\displaystyle E^{a}(v^{i},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm in}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla u(y))\,dy+\frac{c_{3}k^{1/2}}{c_{1}}\int_{Q^{i}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla u(y))\,dy

\displaystyle+C_{1}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(Q^{i})+\int_{Q^{i}_{\rm out}}\hat{f}(y,\nabla u(y))\,dy+\frac{c_{5}+1}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

for some constant
C_{1}
depending only on
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5}
, and
k
.
Summing over
i\in\{1,...,m\}
and dividing by
m
, we obtain

(5.21)

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{a}(v^{i},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{m}\Big)E^{a}(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{C}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

for some constant
C
depending only on
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5}
, and
k
, which we may assume to be larger than the constant of Lemma
5.3
. Finally, using that lemma, we get

(5.22)

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{s}(v^{i},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{m}\Big)E^{s}(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)),

so that combining (
5.21
) with (
5.22
) we obtain that there exists
i\in\{1,...,m\}
such that (
5.16
) holds with
v=v^{i}
.

Suppose now that
\hat{f}
is continuous in
{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
. Thanks to (f2
′
) and to (f4), we have that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
there exists
\omega(x)
which minimizes the continuous function
\xi\mapsto\hat{f}(x,\xi)
.

Since
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{F}
, we also have that

|\omega(x)|\leq\frac{c_{2}+c_{4}}{c_{1}}.

This inequality, together with the uniform continuity of
\hat{f}
on compact sets of
{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, implies that there exists a
\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m}(x)>0
such that for every
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\lambda,\nu}_{m}(x))
one has

(5.23)

|\hat{f}(y,\xi)-\hat{f}(x,\xi)|\leq\frac{1}{m}

for every
y\in Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
and
|\xi|\leq(c_{2}+c_{4})/c_{1}
. In particular, we have

(5.24)

|\hat{f}(y,\omega(x))-\hat{f}(x,\omega(x))|\leq\frac{1}{m}.

Exploiting these inequalities and the minimality of
\omega(x)
, we obtain that

(5.25)

\hat{f}(y,\omega(x))\leq\hat{f}(y,\xi)+\frac{2}{m}

for every
y\in Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
.
Indeed, if
|\xi|\leq(c_{2}+c_{4})/c_{1}
, we apply (
5.23
) twice to get

\hat{f}(y,\omega(x))\leq\hat{f}(x,\omega(x))+\frac{1}{m}\leq\hat{f}(x,\xi)+\frac{1}{m}\leq\hat{f}(y,\xi)+\frac{2}{m}.

If
|\xi|>(c_{4}+c_{2})/c_{1}
, recalling the minimality of
\omega(x)
, by (f3) and (f2
′
) we have
\hat{f}(x,\omega(x))\leq\hat{f}(x,0)\leq c_{4}\leq\hat{f}(y,\xi)
, which, togheter with (
5.24
), implies (
5.25
). As in the previous part of the proof, for every
y\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
we denote
w(y):=\omega(x)(y-x)+\ell_{\xi}(x)
. We can replace (
5.19
) by (
5.25
) in the argument that we used in the case where
\hat{f}
was not assumed to be continuous on
{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
and this leads to the existence of
i\in\{1,...,m\}
such that

E(w+\psi^{i}_{R}\circ(u-w),Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{m}\Big)E(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{C}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

In the case where
\hat{f}
does not depend on
x
the same is true for
\omega
and (
5.25
) holds for every
\rho>0
, since it is a direct consequence of the minimality.
∎

The next result follows immediately from Lemma
5.9
. Given
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, we set

(5.26)

C_{\xi}:=2c_{3}k^{1/2}C|\xi|+2C(c_{4}+1),

where
C
is the constant of Lemma
5.9
.
We also recall that the constant
c_{\xi,m}
is given by (
5.14
).

Corollary 5.10
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
and assume that there exists a function
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{F}
satisfying (
5.15
). Then there exists a set
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N)=0
, such that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
\lambda\geq 1
there exists
\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,\xi}(x)>0
such that for every
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,\xi}(x))
there exists
u\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}u={\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}}\ell_{\xi}
and
\|u-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq c_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho
, such that

(5.27)

E(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{C_{\xi}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{m}.

In particular for
t=c_{\xi,m}\lambda
, we have

(5.28)

m_{t\rho}^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{C_{\xi}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{m}.

Moreover, if
\hat{f}
is continuous in
{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
then
N=\varnothing
. Finally, if
\hat{f}
is independent on
x
then
\rho^{\eta,\lambda}_{\xi,\nu}(x)=+\infty
.

Proof.

Let
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
and
\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,\xi}(x)>0
be as in Lemma
5.9
. Consider a
v\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}v=\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
, such that

E(v,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{m}.

Since the function
\ell_{\xi}
is a competitor for the minimisation problem in the right-hand side of the previous inequality, we also get

E(v,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4}+1)\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

Thus, we may apply Lemma
5.9
to obtain a function
u\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}u={\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}}\ell_{\xi}
and
\|u-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq c_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho
, such that (
5.27
) holds, concluding the proof.
∎

The following proposition shows that under the hypotheses of Lemma
5.9
the functions
f_{\delta}
and
f^{\infty}_{\delta}
converge
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e. as
\delta\to 0^{+}
to
f
and
f^{\infty}
, respectively.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
. Assume that there exists
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{F}
such that (
5.15
) holds. Then

(5.29)

\displaystyle\displaystyle f(x,\xi)=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}f_{\delta}(x,\xi)\quad{\rm\,for\,\,}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}\textup{-}{\rm a.e}.\,x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}{\rm\,\,and\,\,for\,\,every}\,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d},

(5.30)

\displaystyle\displaystyle f^{\infty}(x,\xi)=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}f^{\infty}_{\delta}(x,\xi)\quad{\rm for\,\,}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}\textup{-}{\rm a.e.}\,x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}{\rm\,\,and\,\,for\,\,every}\,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}.

Proof.

From Remark
5.8
, we deduce that the limits in the right-hand side of (
5.29
) and of (
5.30
) exist and that

\displaystyle\displaystyle f(x,\xi)\leq\inf_{\delta>0}f_{\delta}(x,\xi),

\displaystyle\displaystyle f^{\infty}(x,\xi)\leq\inf_{\delta>0}f^{\infty}_{\delta}(x,\xi).

We are left with proving that the converse inequality also holds. Let
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-negligible set of Corollary
5.10
, let
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, and
t\geq 1
.
By (
5.9
), for
\rho>0
small enough we have

(5.31)

\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{t\rho^{d}}\leq\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}+\frac{1}{tm}.

We can now apply Corollary
5.10
, with
\xi
replaced by
t\xi
, to obtain a function
u\in BV(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}t\ell_{\xi}
and
\|u-t\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq c_{t\xi,m}\rho
, such that (
5.27
) holds with
\xi
replaced by
t\xi
. By taking
\rho>0
small enough, we may also suppose that
\|u-t\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq 1/2
. Inequality (
5.31
) then yields

\frac{E(u,Q(x,\rho))}{t\rho^{d}}\leq\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}+\frac{C_{t\xi}+1}{tm}\leq\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}+\frac{C_{\xi}+1}{m},

where
C_{\xi}>0
is the constant given by (
5.26
). Note that in the last inequality we have used the estimate
C_{t\xi}\leq tC_{\xi}
for every
t\geq 1
.

We now compare
E_{\delta}(u,Q(x,\rho))
and
E(u,Q(x,\rho))
. Since
\|u-t\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq 1/2
, we have
|[u]|\leq 1

{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}
-a.e. on
J_{u}
. Hence, by (c1
′
) we get

\displaystyle E_{\delta}(u,Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(u,Q(x,\rho))+\delta|Du|(Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E(u,Q(x,\rho))+\delta\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\nabla u|\,dx+\delta|D^{c}u|(Q(x,\rho))+\delta\int_{J_{u}}|[u]|\land 1\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{\delta}{c_{1}}\Big)E(u,Q(x,\rho))+\frac{c_{2}\delta}{c_{1}}\rho^{d}.

Letting
\delta
be so small that
\delta/c_{1}\leq 1/m
and
\delta c_{2}/c_{1}\leq 1/m,
we then obtain

\frac{E_{\delta}(u,Q(x,\rho))}{t\rho^{d}}\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)\frac{E(u,Q(x,\rho))}{t\rho^{d}}+\frac{1}{m}\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)\Big(\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}+\frac{C_{\xi}+1}{m}\Big)

and, recalling that
\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}t\ell_{\xi}
, we get

\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(\ell_{t\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{t\rho^{d}}\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)\Big(\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}+\frac{C_{\xi}+1}{m}\Big).

Evaluating this last inequality at
t=1
and taking the
\limsup
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, we deduce

f_{\delta}(x,\xi)\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)\Big(f(x,\xi)+\frac{C_{\xi}+1}{m}\Big),

while taking the
\limsup
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
first and letting then
t\to+\infty
, we obtain

f^{\infty}_{\delta}(x,\xi)\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)\Big(f^{\infty}(x,\xi)+\frac{C_{\xi}+1}{m}\Big).

Finally, letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
first and then taking the limit for
m\to+\infty
, we conclude the proof.
∎

We now pass to the study of the minimisation problems used to define the integrands for the surface terms.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
,

x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\rho>0
. Then there exists
u\in BV(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
and
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq\sigma^{m}
, such that

(5.32)

E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{K(|\zeta|\land 1)}{m}\rho^{d-1}+K\rho^{d},

where
K:=\max\{c_{3}kC,(C+c_{4}+1)\}
and
C
is the constant of (g) of Definition
3.8
.

Proof.

By (
5.7
) there exists
v\in BV(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
, such that

(5.33)

E(v,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\rho^{d}\leq c_{3}k(|\zeta|\land 1)\rho^{d-1}+\rho^{d},

where the second inequality follows from (c2
′
) and the fact that
u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
is a competitor for the minimisation problem.

Let us fix
R\geq|\zeta|
. Note that with this choice of
R
by (
3.4
) we have
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
and
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq\sigma^{m}
. By property (g) of Definition
3.8
and (
5.33
) there exists
i\in\{1,...,m\}
such that
u:=\psi_{R}^{i}\circ v
satisfies

\displaystyle E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E(v,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\,dx+c_{4}\rho^{d}+\frac{C}{m}\left(E(v,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\rho^{d}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+(C+c_{4}+1)\rho^{d}+\frac{c_{3}kC(|\zeta|\land 1)}{m}\rho^{d-1},

concluding the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove that
g_{\delta}
converges to
g
.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},

\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k},
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Then

(5.34)

g(x,\zeta,\nu)=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu).

Proof.

Thanks to Remark
5.8
, the limit in the right-hand side of (
5.34
) exists and

g(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu).

We now prove that the converse inequality holds true as well. Let us fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. By (
5.11
) for
\rho>0
small enough we have that

(5.35)

\frac{m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\leq g(x,\zeta,\nu)+\frac{1}{m}.

We can now apply Lemma
5.12
to obtain a function
u\in BV(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
and

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq\sigma^{m}
, such that

E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu}.Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{K}{m}\rho^{d-1}+K\rho^{d}.

From (
5.35
) we then deduce that

\frac{E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\leq g(x,\zeta,\nu)+\frac{K+1}{m}+K\rho.

To conclude, we compare
E_{\delta}(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
with
E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
. Since
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq\sigma^{m}
, we have
|[u]|\leq 2\sigma^{m}|[u]|\land 1.
Hence, we get

\displaystyle E_{\delta}(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\delta|Du|(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\delta\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\!\!\!\!\!|\nabla u|\,dx+\delta|D^{c}u|(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+2\delta\sigma^{m}\int_{J_{u}}\!\!\!\!\!|[u]|\land 1\,{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{2\delta\sigma^{m}}{c_{1}}\Big)E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{2c_{2}\delta\sigma^{m}}{c_{1}}\rho^{d}.

Letting
\delta
be so small that
2\delta\sigma^{m}/c_{1}\leq 1/m
and
2c_{2}\delta\sigma^{m}/c_{1}\leq 1
, we get

\frac{E_{\delta}(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)g(x,\zeta,\nu)+\frac{K+1}{m}+\Big(1+K\Big)\rho.

Recalling the definition of
m^{E_{\delta}}
, from this last estimate we deduce that

\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)g(x,\zeta,\nu)+\frac{K+1}{m}+\Big(1+K\Big)\rho.

Taking the limsup as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, we obtain

g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq\Big(1+\frac{1}{m}\Big)g(x,\zeta,\nu)+\frac{K+1}{m}.

Taking the
\limsup
for
\delta\to 0^{+}
first, and the limit for
m\to+\infty
then,
we conclude the proof.
∎

As the following proposition shows, the functions
f
and
g
defined by (
5.9
) and (
5.11
), belong to
\mathcal{F}
and to
\mathcal{G}
, respectively.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
,
f
,
\hat{f}
, and
g
be the functions defined by (
5.9
), (
5.13
), and (
5.11
), respectively. Then
\hat{f},f\in\mathcal{F}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

Proof.

The proof of the inclusions
\hat{f}
,
f\in\mathcal{F}
and of properties (g1)-(g4) for
g
, can be obtained by adapting the arguments of
[
21
, Section 5]
, with minor changes.

We are left with proving that
g
satisfies (g5). To see this, let
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\setminus\{0\}
with
c_{6}k|\zeta_{1}|\leq|\zeta_{2}|
. We set
\lambda:=|\zeta_{1}|/|\zeta_{2}|
and note that
\lambda\leq 1/(c_{6}k)
. Let
R\in SO(k)
be a rotation that maps
\lambda\zeta_{2}
to
\zeta_{1}
. Let
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. By (
5.11
) there exists
u\in BV(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho));{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta_{2},\nu}
, such that

(5.36)

E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta_{2},\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\rho^{d}.

We set
v:=\lambda Ru
and note that
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}v=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta_{1},\nu}
. Then, by means of (h) of Definition
3.8
, we have

E(v,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq E(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+(c_{4}+c_{2})\rho^{d},

so that, by (
5.36
), we infer

(5.37)

E(v,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(u_{x,\zeta_{2},\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+(c_{4}+c_{2}+1)\rho^{d}.

Dividing this last inequality by
\rho^{d-1}
and taking the
\limsup
for
{\rho\to 0^{+}}
, we obtain

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{E(v,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}\leq g(x,\zeta_{2},\nu),

which, in light of the fact that
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}v=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta_{1},\nu}
, implies

g(x,\zeta_{1},\nu)\leq g(x,\zeta_{2},\nu),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Using the results of
[
9
]
, we can now establish an integral representation both for the absolutely continuous part and the jump part of the perturbed functionals
E_{\delta}
. This representation is achieved by means of the functions
f_{\delta}
and
g_{\delta}
defined above.

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, and
\delta>0
. Let
E_{\delta}^{a}
and
E^{j}_{\delta}
be the functionals introduced in Definition
5.4
and let
f_{\delta}
and
g_{\delta}
be the functions defined by (
5.10
) and (
5.12
), respectively. Then

(5.38)

\displaystyle\displaystyle E^{a}_{\delta}(u,B)=\int_{B}f_{\delta}(x,\nabla u)\,dx,

(5.39)

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{\delta}^{j}(u,B)=\int_{B\cap J_{u}}g_{\delta}(x,[u],\nu)\,{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1},

for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

Proof.

The result follows from
[
9
]
, using the same arguments of
[
21
, Theorem 6.1]
.
∎

With this proposition at hand, we are ready to prove the integral representation of
E^{a}
and of
E^{j}
.

Theorem 5.16
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}),
and let
f
and
g
be the functions defined by (
5.9
) and (
5.11
), respectively. Then

(5.40)

\displaystyle\displaystyle E^{a}(u,B)=\int_{B}f(x,\nabla u)\,dx,

(5.41)

\displaystyle\displaystyle E^{j}(u,B)=\int_{J_{u}\cap B}g(x,[u],\nu)\,{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}

for every
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{f}
be the function defined by (
5.13
). We first show that

(5.42)

E^{a}(u,B)=\int_{B}\hat{f}(x,\nabla u)\,dx\,\,\,\text{ for every $u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})$ and $B\in\mathcal{B}(A)$.}

To this aim, we begin noting that
E^{a}_{\delta}(u,B)=E^{a}(u,B)+\delta\int_{B}|\nabla u|
for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
, so that

E^{a}(u,B)=\inf_{\delta>0}E_{\delta}^{a}(u,B)=\lim_{\delta>0}E_{\delta}^{a}(u,B).

By definition of
f_{\delta}
, we have that

f_{\delta}(x,\xi)\leq(c_{3}k^{1/2}+\delta)|\xi|+c_{4},

for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, so that by invoking the Dominate Convergence Theorem,
we obtain (
5.42
).

We now prove that

(5.43)

E^{j}(u,B)=\int_{B}g(x,[u],\nu_{u})\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}\,\,\,\text{ for every $u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})$ and $B\in\mathcal{B}(A)$.}

To show this, note that
E_{\delta}^{j}(u,B)=E^{j}(u,B)+\delta\int_{J_{u}\cap B}|[u]|d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}
for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
; therefore,

E^{j}(u,B)=\inf_{\delta>0}E_{\delta}^{j}(u,B)=\lim_{\delta>0}E_{\delta}^{j}(u,B).

It is immediate to see that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have

g_{\delta}(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq(c_{3}k+\delta)|\zeta|\land 1.

Hence, recalling Proposition
5.13
and using (
5.39
), by the Dominated Convergence Theorem we obtain (
5.41
).

Consider now
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Let
R_{m}>0
with
R_{m}\to+\infty
. . By Lemma
5.2
, both (
5.2
) and (
5.4
) hold for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
. To conclude, it is enough to show that

(5.44)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{a}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,A)=\int_{A}f(x,\nabla u)\,dx,

(5.45)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{m\to+\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{j}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,A)=\int_{J_{u}\cap A}g(x,[u],\nu_{u})\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}.

Since for every
i\in\{1,...m\}
we have that
\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, from (
5.40
) and (
5.43
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\displaystyle E^{a}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,A)=\int_{A}f(x,\nabla(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u))\,dx,

\displaystyle\displaystyle E^{j}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,A)=\int_{J_{\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}(u)}\cap A}\!\!\!g(x,[\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u],\nu_{u})\,{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}.

By (
3.4
) we have
|\nabla(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u)|\leq|\nabla u|
for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
i\in\{1,...,m\}
. Morover,
\nabla u\in L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d})
by Proposition
2.4
(a). For every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists
i(m),j(m)\in\{1,...,m\}
such that

\int_{A}f(x,\nabla(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i(m)}\circ u))\,dx\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{a}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,A)\leq\int_{A}f(x,\nabla(\psi_{R_{m}}^{j(m)}\circ u))\,dx.

We observe that both
\nabla(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i(m)}\circ u)
and
\nabla(\psi_{R_{m}}^{j(m)}\circ u)
converge to
\nabla u
pointwise
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e. in
A
as
m\to+\infty
. Hence, recalling that by Proposition
5.14
the function
f
belongs to
\mathcal{F}
, the Dominated Convergence Theorem implies (
5.44
).

As for (
5.45
), by Proposition
2.4
(d), for every
i\in\{1,...,m\}
we have that
|[\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u]|\land 1\leq|[u]|\land 1
and that
[\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u](x)\to[u](x)
for
{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in J_{u}
. For every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
we choose
i(m),j(m)\in\{1,...,m\}
such that

\int_{J_{u}\cap A}g(x,[\psi_{R_{m}}^{i(m)}\circ u],\nu_{u})\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{j}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u,A)\leq\int_{J_{u}\cap A}g(x,[\psi_{R_{m}}^{j(m)}\circ u],\nu_{u})\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}.

Since by Proposition
5.14
the function
g
belongs to
\mathcal{G}
, an application of the Dominated Convergence Theorem yields (
5.45
), concluding the proof.
∎

6.
A smaller collection of integrands

As the scalar case studied in
[
21
]
and
[
20
]
suggests, to recover a full integral representation for functionals in
\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
, it is convenient to consider a smaller collection of integrands, whose definition is closely related to those studied in
[
15
]
. In particular, we will show that the Cantor part
E^{c}
can be represented as an integral functional whenever
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
is the
\Gamma
-limit of a sequence of functionals associated to integrands in this in this smaller class and the volume integrand corresponding to
E
does not depend on
x
(see Theorem
7.1
).

In the rest of the paper we fix two new constants
c_{7}>0
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Moreover, we fix a continuous non-decreasing function
\vartheta\colon[0,+\infty)\to[0,+\infty)
such that

(6.1)

\vartheta(0)=0\,\,\,\textup{ and }\,\,\,\vartheta(t)\geq\frac{c_{1}}{c_{3}}t-1\quad\text{for every $t\geq 0$}.

The smaller collection of volume integrands is introduced in the following definition.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
be the collection of functions
f\in\mathcal{F}
such that

(6.2)

\Big|\frac{f(x,s\xi)}{s}-\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}\Big|\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}f(x,s\xi)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}f(x,t\xi)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}

for
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and for every
s,t>0
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
.

Remark 6.2
.

Arguing as in
[
20
, Remark 4.3]
, one can show that
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
if and only if for
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
we have

f^{\infty}(x,\xi)=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{1}{s}f(x,s\xi),

and

\Big|\frac{1}{s}f(x,s\xi)-f^{\infty}(x,\xi)\Big|\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}f(x,s\xi)^{1-\alpha}

for
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
and for every
s>0
. This is closely related to condition (H4) of
[
9
]
.

We now introduce the smaller collection of surface integrands.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
be the collection of functions
g\in\mathcal{G}
such that

\Big|\frac{g(x,s\zeta,\nu)}{s}-\frac{g(x,t\zeta,\nu)}{t}\Big|\leq\vartheta(s|\zeta|)\frac{g(x,s\zeta,\nu)}{s}+\vartheta(t|\zeta|)\frac{g(x,t,\nu)}{t},

for every
s,t>0
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

Remark 6.4
.

The arguments of
[
20
, Remark 4.5]
show that
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
if and only if for
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have that the limit

(6.3)

\displaystyle g^{0}(x,\zeta,\nu):=\lim_{s\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{s}g(x,s\zeta,\nu)

exists and

\Big|\frac{1}{s}g(x,s\zeta,\nu)-g^{0}(x,\zeta,\nu)\Big|\leq\vartheta(s|\zeta|)\frac{1}{s}g(x,s\zeta,\nu)\leq c_{3}k\vartheta(s|\zeta|)|\zeta|

for every
s>0
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. By
[
15
, Remark 3.5]
this is closely related to condition (g5) of that paper.

Remark 6.5
.

The class
\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
is non-empty, since the function
(x,\zeta,\nu)\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{k}((c_{1}|\zeta_{i}|)\land c_{3})
belongs to
\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
. Arguing as in
[
20
, Remark 4.7]
, it is possible to show that
\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}\neq\mathchar 31\relax
if and only if
\vartheta
satisfies (
6.1
)
.

As in Section
3
, we introduce a space of functionals
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
containing
E^{f,g}
for every
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
.

Definition 6.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
be the space of functionals
E\in\mathfrak{E}
satisfying the condition

(6.4)

\Big|\frac{E(su,A)}{s}-\frac{E(tu,A)}{t}\Big|\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(su,A)^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta(su^{A})\frac{E(su,A)}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(tu,A)^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta(tu^{A})\frac{E(tu,A)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A),

for every
t,s>0
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, and
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, where
u^{A}:=\textup{osc}_{A}u:=\textup{ess}\sup_{x,y\in A}|u(x)-u(y)|
. We also set
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{\rm sc}:=\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}\cap\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}.

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
. Then the functional
E^{f,g}
of Definition
3.7
belongs to
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
.

Proof.

The result is proved as in
[
20
, Proposition 4.9]
, replacing Remark 2.9 by our Proposition
3.13
.
∎

To study the
\Gamma
-limit of sequences of functionals
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
, given
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
and a function
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, it is important to be able to find approximate recovery sequences for
u
which are bounded in
L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. This is taken care of in the next lemma.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
,
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
,
E\in\mathfrak{E}
,
and
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}
, with
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converging to
E
in the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.
Then there exist sequences
(u_{n})_{n}\subset BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and
(v_{n})_{n}\subset BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
converging to
u
in
L^{1}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
such that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\displaystyle u^{A}_{n}\leq 4\sigma^{m}u^{A}\quad\text{ and }\quad\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq 2\sigma^{m}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\quad\text{for every }n\in{\mathbb{N}},

(6.6)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(u_{n},A)\lor\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(v_{n},A)\leq E(u,A)+C\frac{E(u,A)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)}{m},

where
C
is the constant in property (g) of Definition
3.8
.

Proof.

By property (d) of Definition
3.8
, it is not restrictive to assume that
u^{A}=\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}
. By
\Gamma
-convergence there exists a sequence
(w_{n})\subset L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
converging to
u
in
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
such that

(6.7)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(w_{n},A)=E(u,A).

Let us fix
R:=2\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}
. By property (g) of Definition
3.8
for every
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists an index
i(n)\in\{1,...,m\}
such that

E_{n}(\psi_{R}^{i(n)}\circ w_{n},A)\leq E_{n}(w_{n},A)+C\frac{E(u,A)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)}{m}+c_{4}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A^{R}_{v_{n},0}).

Since
w_{n}\to u
in
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, by our choice of
R
we have
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A_{w_{n},0}^{R})\to 0
. Setting
u_{n}:=\psi_{R}^{i(n)}\circ w_{n}
, we deduce from (
6.7
) and the previous inequality that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(u_{n},A)\leq E(u,A)+C\frac{E(u,A)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)}{m}.

We conclude noting that by (
3.4
) we have
u_{n}^{A}\leq 2\|u_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq 4\sigma^{m}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}=4\sigma^{m}u^{A}
and
u_{n}\to u
in
L^{1}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.

The construction of
v_{n}
is similar.
∎

We now want study
\Gamma
-limits of sequences of functionals in
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
. To this aim, it is convenient to introduce a family of subspaces of
\mathfrak{E}
, in which (
6.4
) holds in a weaker form.
Given
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we set

(6.8)

\vartheta_{m}(t):=\vartheta(4\sigma^{m}t)

for every
t\geq 0
.

Definition 6.9
.

Given
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we denote by
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}
the space of functionals
E\in\mathfrak{E}
satisfying the condition

(6.9)

\Big|\frac{E(su,A)}{s}-\frac{E(tu,A)}{t}\Big|\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(su,A)^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{m}(su^{A})\frac{E(su,A)}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)\\
\hskip-42.67912pt\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(tu,A)^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{m}(tu^{A})\frac{E(tu,A)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)+R_{m,s}(u,A)+R_{m,t}(u,A)

for every
t,s>0
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
,
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A,{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, where
\vartheta_{m}
is the function defined by (
6.8
) and for every
t>0

(6.10)

\hskip-5.69046ptR_{m,t}(u,A):=C\frac{1+\vartheta_{m}(tu^{A})}{t}\frac{E(tu,A)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)}{m}+C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{t}\Big(\frac{E(tu,A)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)}{m}\Big)^{1-\alpha}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha},

C
being the constant of property (g) of Definition
3.8
.
We also set
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}:=\Big(\bigcap_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}\Big)\cap\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
.

We now show that
\Gamma
-limits of sequences in
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
belong to the larger space
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
(E_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of functionals in
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
and let
E\in\mathfrak{E}
. Assume that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E(\cdot,A)
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Then
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}\cap\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. In particular,
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
.

Proof.

By the semicontinuity of
\Gamma
-limits we have
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
. Thus, given
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we only need to prove that
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}
.

By the continuity of
\vartheta_{m}
and exchanging the roles of
s
and
t
, to conclude it is enough to show that

(6.11)

(1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{A}))\frac{E(su,A)}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(su,A)^{1-\alpha}\\
\leq(1+\vartheta_{m}(tu^{A}))\frac{E(tu,A)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(tu,A)^{1-\alpha}+R_{m,t}(u,A),

assuming that the left-hand side is stricty positive.

Let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
,
u\in L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, and
t,s>0
. By Lemma
6.8
, there exists a sequence
(u_{n})_{n}\subset BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
such that
u_{n}\to u
in
L^{1}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
,
u_{n}^{A}\leq 4\sigma^{m}u^{A}
, and

(6.12)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(tu_{n},A)\leq E(tu,A)+C\frac{E(tu,A)+{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)}{m}.

Since
u_{n}\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, by (
6.4
) we get

(1-\vartheta(su_{n}^{A}))\frac{E_{n}(su_{n},A)}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E_{n}(su_{n},A)^{1-\alpha}\\
\leq(1+\vartheta(tu_{n}^{A}))\frac{E_{n}(tu_{n},A)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E_{n}(tu_{n},A)^{1-\alpha}.

Taking the limsup as
n\to+\infty
and using the monotonicity of
\vartheta
and (
6.12
), we deduce that

(6.13)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big((1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{A}))\frac{E_{n}(su_{n},A)}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E_{n}(su_{n},A)^{1-\alpha}\Big)\\
\leq\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big((1-\vartheta(su_{n}^{A}))\frac{E_{n}(su_{n},A)}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E_{n}(su_{n},A)^{1-\alpha}\Big)\\
\leq\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big(1+\vartheta(tu_{n}^{A}))\frac{E_{n}(tu_{n},A)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E_{n}(tu_{n},A)^{1-\alpha}\Big)\\
\leq(1+\vartheta_{m}(tu^{A}))\frac{E(tu,A)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)+\frac{c_{7}}{t}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{\alpha}E(tu,A)^{1-\alpha}+R_{m,t}(u,A),

where
R_{m,t}(u,A)
is defined by (
6.10
).
To deal with the first term in the previous chain of inequalities we introduce the function
\Phi
defined for every
z\in[0,+\infty)
as

\Phi(z):=(1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{A}))\frac{z}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)^{1-\alpha}z^{1-\alpha}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A).

Since the left-hand side of (
6.11
) is strictly positive, we have that
\Phi(E(su,A))>0
, which implies
(1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{A}))>0
. We set
z_{0}:=c_{7}^{1/\alpha}(1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{A}))^{-1/\alpha}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A)
and observe that
\Phi
is increasing on
(z_{0},+\infty)
and that if
\Phi(z)>0
then
z>z_{0}
; in particular,
E(su,A)>z_{0}
.
Finally, from the
\Gamma
-convergence of
E_{n}(\cdot,A)
to
E(\cdot,A)
and from the convergence of
u_{n}
to
u
in
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, we deduce that

E(su,A)\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(su_{n},A),

which, by the monotonicity of
\Phi
, implies

\Phi(E(su,A))\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\Phi(E_{n}(su_{n},A)).

Recalling the definition of
\Phi
, from this inequality and
(
6.13
) we obtain
(
6.11
), concluding the proof.
∎

The next technical results will used in the proof of the representation theorem presented in Section
7
. Given
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, and
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we set

(6.14)

\kappa_{\xi,m}:=2c_{\xi,m}+2d^{1/2}|\xi|,\\

where
c_{\xi,m}
the constant defined by (
5.14
).

Lemma 6.11
.

For every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
there exists a constant with the following property: for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}\cap\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
there exists a set Let
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}\cap\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
. Then
there exists
N_{m}\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}),
with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N_{m})=0
, satisfying the following property: for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N_{m}
,
\lambda\geq 1
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
s,t>0
, and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
there exist
\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,\xi,t,s}(x)>0
, and
M_{\xi}
, the latter depending only on
|\xi|
and on the structurual constants
c_{1},...,c_{7}
,
k
and
\alpha
, such that for every
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,\xi,t,s}(x))
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}\Big|

\displaystyle\,\,\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{d\alpha}m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{d\alpha}m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(s\land 1)m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{s}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

(6.15)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(t\land 1)m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

where
\kappa_{\xi,m}
is the constant defined by (
6.14
). If, in addition the function
f
defined by (
5.9
) does not depend on
x
, the set
N_{m}=\mathchar 31\relax
and
\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,\xi,t,s}(x)=+\infty
.

Proof.

By hypothesis
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
, so that by Theorem
5.16
, the function
f
defined by (
5.9
) satisfies (
5.15
) for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
and
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Hence, we may apply Corollary
5.10
to obtain a set
N_{m}\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N_{m})=0
, satisfying the following property: for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N_{m}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
t>0
,
\nu\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
, and
\lambda\geq 1
there exists
\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,t\xi}(x)
such that for every
\rho\in(0,\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,t\xi}(x))
, there exists
u\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with tr
{}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=
tr
{}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
and
\|u-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq\frac{1}{t}c_{t\xi,m}\lambda\rho\leq\frac{1}{t\land 1}c_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho
, such that

(6.16)

E(tu,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{C_{t\xi}}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}\leq m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{(t\lor 1)C_{\xi}}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

where
C_{\xi}
is defined by (
5.26
). Note that the oscillation of
u
satisfies

(6.17)

u^{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\leq(\tfrac{2}{t\land 1}c_{\xi,m}+\tfrac{2}{t\land 1}d^{1/2}|\xi|)\lambda\rho=\frac{\kappa_{\xi,m}}{t\land 1}\lambda\rho.

We can estimate
m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
by evaluating
E(\cdot,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
at
t\ell_{\xi}
. Recalling (
6.16
) and (c2
′
) of Definition
3.8
, this leads to

(6.18)

E(tu,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\big(c_{3}k^{1/2}t|\xi|+c_{4}+(t\lor 1)C_{\xi}\big)\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

Since
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}
, by (
6.9
) for every
s>0
we have that

\displaystyle(1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}))\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}(\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d})^{\alpha}E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\qquad-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}-R_{m,s}(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq(1+\vartheta_{m}(tu^{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}))\frac{E(tu,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}(\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d})^{\alpha}E(tu,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}

(6.19)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\qquad+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+R_{m,t}(u,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)),

where
R
is defined by (
6.10
). Using the monotonicity of
\vartheta_{m}
, (
6.16
), (
6.17
), (
6.18
), and the subadditivity of the function
z\mapsto z^{1-\alpha}
on
[0,+\infty)
we see that there exists a positive constant
M_{\xi}
, independent of
m
, such that the left-hand side can be bounded from below by

\displaystyle\Big(1-\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}-C\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{sm}

(6.20)

\displaystyle-C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{s}\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}+\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{m^{1-\alpha}},

while the right-hand side of (
6.19
) can be bounded from above by

\displaystyle\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{C(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4}+C_{\xi}+1)+C_{\xi}}{(t\land 1)m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

(6.21)

\displaystyle+\Big(C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{t^{\alpha}}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\Big)\frac{\big(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4}+C_{\xi}\big)^{1-\alpha}+1+C_{\xi}^{1-\alpha}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

Therefore, by (
6.19
)-(
6.21
) there exists a constant
M_{\xi}
, independent of
m
, such that

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\Big(1-\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt-C\Big(1{+}\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)){+}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{sm}-C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{s}\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}{+}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{m^{1-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(t\land 1)m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

We claim that the previous inequality still holds replacing
E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
by
m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
.
To prove the claim, for given
s,t>0
we introduce the function
\Phi
defined for every
z\in[0,+\infty)
as

\Phi(z):=\Big(1-\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{z}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}z^{1-\alpha}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}\\
-C\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda}{t\land 1}\rho\Big)\Big)\frac{z+\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{sm}-C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{s}\frac{z^{1-\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}+\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}{m^{1-\alpha}},

so that the left-hand side of (
6.20
) is equal to
\Phi(E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
.
We now show that

(6.22)

\Phi(m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)))\leq\Phi(E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))).

Since the righ-hand side is clearly larger than zero, it is enough to prove this inequality when

\Phi(m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)))>0
. Note that this positivity condition implies that
1-\vartheta_{m}(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1})>0
. Some straightforward computations, show that if
\Phi(z)>0
then
z>z_{0}
, where

z_{0}:=c_{7}^{1/\alpha}\Big((1-\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)-\Big(\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{C}{m}\Big)^{-1/\alpha}\Big(1+\frac{C^{\alpha}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\Big)^{1/\alpha}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

One can also see that
\Phi
is increasing on
(z_{0},+\infty)
.
Since
\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}su=\text{tr}_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}s\ell_{\xi}
, we obtain that

(6.23)

m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)))\leq E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)).

Since we assumed that
\Phi(m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)))>0
, we have that
m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))>z_{0}
. Hence, recalling that
\Phi
is increasing on
(z_{0},+\infty)
, from (
6.23
) we obtain that (
6.22
). Thus, in (
6.20
) we can substitute
E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
by
m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))
. This new inequality, together with

\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}\leq\frac{c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4}}{s\land 1}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

implies that

\displaystyle\Big(1-\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}-\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(s\land 1)m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}-\frac{1}{s}\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(d-1)}\rho^{\alpha d}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho}{t\land 1}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(t\land 1)m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}+\Big(\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

Exchanging the roles of
s
and
t
, this gives (
6.15
).

If, in addition, the function
f
defined by (
5.9
) does not depend on
x
, then in Corollary
5.10
we have
N_{m}=\mathchar 31\relax
and
\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,t\xi}(x)=\rho^{\nu,\lambda}_{m,s\xi}(x)=+\infty
, concluding the proof in this case.
∎

The following lemma deals with the case with boundary conditions related to the the jump functions
u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
.

Lemma 6.12
.

There exists a positive constant
M>0
, depending only on the structural constants
c_{1},...,c_{7}
,
k
, and on
\alpha
, such that for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}\cap\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
s,t>0
, and
\rho>0
we have

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Big|\frac{m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d\alpha}m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|)\frac{m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\quad\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d\alpha}m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|)\frac{m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\Big(\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}\rho^{d-1}+\frac{M}{sm}\rho^{d}\Big)+\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}\frac{M|\zeta|^{1-\alpha}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d-1+\alpha}+\frac{1}{s}\frac{M}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d}

(6.24)

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\Big(\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}\rho^{d-1}+\frac{M}{tm}\rho^{d}\Big)+\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}\frac{M|\zeta|^{1-\alpha}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d-1+\alpha}+\frac{1}{t}\frac{M}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d}.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.12
for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
,
t>0
,
\nu\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
, and
\rho>0
there exists a function
u\in BV(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})\cap L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
such that
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}u_{x,\zeta,\nu}
,
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq\sigma^{m}|\zeta|
, and

(6.25)

E(tu,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\frac{tK|\zeta|}{m}\rho^{d-1}+K\rho^{d},

where
K>0
is the constant in Lemma
5.12
.
Note that the oscillation of
u
satisfies
u^{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\leq 2\sigma^{m}|\zeta|
. We now estimate
m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
by evaluating
E(\cdot,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
at
tu_{x,\zeta,\nu}
and by (c2) we get

m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq c_{3}kt|\zeta|\rho^{d-1}+c_{4}\rho^{d},

so that from (
6.25
) we deduce

(6.26)

E(tu,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq t|\zeta|\Big(c_{3}k+\frac{K}{m}\Big)\rho^{d-1}+(c_{4}+K)\rho^{d}.

Since
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta_{m}}_{m}
, by (
6.9
) for every
s>0
we have that

(1-\vartheta_{m}(su^{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}))\frac{E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{\alpha d}E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d}-R_{s}(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\\
\hskip-7.11317pt\leq(1+\vartheta_{m}(tu^{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}))\frac{E(tu,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{\alpha d}E(tu,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d}+R_{t}(u,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)).

Using the inequality
u^{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\leq 2\sigma^{m}|\zeta|
, the monotonicity of
\vartheta_{m}
, and the subadditivity of
z\mapsto z^{1-\alpha}
, we deduce that the left-hand side of the last inequality can be estimated from below by

\displaystyle(1-\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{E(su,Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{\alpha d}E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle-C(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\rho^{d}}{sm}-C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{s}\frac{E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\rho^{\alpha d}+\rho^{d}}{m^{1-\alpha}}.

while the right-hand side can be bounded from above by

(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\rho^{\alpha d}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d}\\
+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{(C+1)|\zeta|(kc_{3}+2K)}{m}\rho^{d-1}+\frac{(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))}{t}\frac{(C+1)(c_{4}+2K+1)}{m}\rho^{d}\\
+\frac{c_{7}}{t^{\alpha}}\frac{{|\zeta|^{1-\alpha}(C^{\alpha}+1)\Big(k^{1-\alpha}c_{3}^{1-\alpha}+2K^{1-\alpha}\Big)}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d-1+\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\frac{(C^{\alpha}+1)(c_{4}^{1-\alpha}+2K^{1-\alpha}+1)}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d},

Therefore, there exists a constant
M>0
such that

\displaystyle(1-\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{\alpha d}E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}-\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle-C(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+\rho^{d}}{sm}-C^{\alpha}\frac{c_{7}}{s}\frac{E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\rho^{\alpha d}+\rho^{d}}{m^{1-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\rho^{\alpha d}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}\rho^{d-1}+\frac{(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))}{t}\frac{M}{m}\rho^{d}+\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}\frac{{M|\zeta|^{1-\alpha}}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d-1+\alpha}+\frac{1}{t}\frac{M}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d},

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.11
we may substitute
E(su,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
by
m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
, so that, taking into account the estimate

m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq c_{3}s|\zeta|\rho^{d-1}+c_{4}\rho^{d},

we obtain

\displaystyle(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}m^{E}(su_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\rho^{\alpha d}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}\rho^{d-1}+\frac{(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))}{s}\frac{M}{m}\rho^{d}+\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}\frac{{M|\zeta|^{1-\alpha}}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d-1+\alpha}+\frac{1}{s}\frac{M}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}m^{E}(tu_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}\rho^{\alpha d}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}\rho^{d-1}+\frac{(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))}{t}\frac{M}{m}\rho^{d}+\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}\frac{{M|\zeta|^{1-\alpha}}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d-1+\alpha}+\frac{1}{t}\frac{M}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d}.

Exchanging the roles of
s,t
, we obtain (
6.24
).
∎

We now investigate the properties of the integrands
f
and
g
associated with functionals in
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
.

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
. Then the function
f
defined by (
5.9
) belongs to
\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
.

Proof.

For every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
let
N_{m}\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
be the
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-negligible set of Lemma
6.11
and let
N:=\bigcup_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}N_{m}
. By the same lemma for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
s,t>0
and every
\rho
small enough we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{s}-\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{t}\Big|

\displaystyle\,\,\leq\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d\alpha}m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{s}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d\alpha}m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))^{1-\alpha}+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa_{\xi,m}\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(s\land 1)m}\rho^{d}+\Big(\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{s}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d}

(6.27)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(1+\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa_{\xi,m}\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{(t\land 1)m}\rho^{d}+\Big(\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\rho^{d},

where
\kappa_{\xi,m}
is the constant defined by (
6.14
).
Since
\vartheta_{m}
is continuous and
\vartheta_{m}(0)=0
, we have that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{s\kappa\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\frac{m^{E}(s\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{s\rho^{d}}\Big)=0,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\vartheta_{m}\Big(\frac{t\kappa\rho}{(s\land 1)(t\land 1)}\Big)\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{t\rho^{d}}\Big)=0.

Therefore, dividing (
6.27
) by
\rho^{d}
and taking the limsup as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{f(x,s\xi)}{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{f(x,t\xi)}{t}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}f(x,s\xi)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{s}+f(x,t\xi)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{M_{\xi}}{(s\land 1)m}+\Big(\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{s}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}+\frac{M_{\xi}}{(t\land 1)m}+\Big(\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}.

Letting
m\to+\infty
and exchanging the roles of
s
and
t
, we recover (
6.2
), which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
s,t>0
. Then the function
g
defined by (
5.11
) satisfies

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{g(x,s\zeta,\nu)}{s}-\frac{g(x,t\zeta,\nu)}{t}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|)\frac{g(x,s\zeta,\nu)}{s}+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|)\frac{g(x,t\zeta,\nu)}{t}

\displaystyle+(2+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|)+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{M|\zeta|}{m},

where
M>0
is the constant of Lemma
6.12
.

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
6.12
, inequality (
6.24
) holds. Dividing this inequality by
\rho^{d-1}
and taking the limsup as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, we immediately get

\displaystyle\frac{g(x,s\zeta,\nu)}{s}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g(x,t\zeta,\nu)}{t}+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|)\frac{g(x,\zeta,\nu)}{s}+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|)\frac{g(x,\zeta,\nu)}{t}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(s|\zeta|))\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}+(1+\vartheta_{2m}(t|\zeta|))\frac{M|\zeta|}{m}.

Exchanging the roles of
s
and
t
we conclude the proof.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

In the scalar case, due to the different nature of the required vertical truncations, it is possible to prove a stronger version of Propositions
6.10
and
6.14
. First, the space
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
turns out to be closed under
\Gamma
-convergence with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
. Then, exploiting this stronger version of Proposition
6.10
, it is shown that the function
g
associated to
E
by (
5.11
) belongs to
\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
.

However, due to the presence of error terms in the truncation procedure, which depend on
\vartheta_{m}
, in the case
k>1
one cannot recover the closedness of
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
and subsequently conclude that
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
.

7.
Full Integral representation

We are finally ready to state and prove the full integral representation result for functionals in the class
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
. More precisely, we will show that if
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
and if the bulk integrand
f
defined by (
5.9
) does not depend on
x
, then the Cantor part
E^{c}
of the functional
E
can be represented by means of
f^{\infty}
. Since this result will be employed to obtain an integral representation for functionals arising from homogenisation of functionals in
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
, the hypothesis that
f
does not depend on
x
is not restrictive for our purposes.

We now state the main result of this section.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
and
f
and
g
be defined by (
5.9
) and by (
5.11
), respectively. Assume that there exists a function
\hat{f}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
such that

(7.1)

f(x,\xi)=\hat{f}(\xi)\quad\text{for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\text{ and for every }\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}.

Then
E=E^{f,g}
, where
E^{f,g}
is the functional introduced in Definition
3.7
.

To prove this result, we try to characterise the Radon-Nikodým derivative of the measures
E^{c}(u,\cdot)
with respect to
|D^{c}u|
for any
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
with
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
. This is done via a careful truncation procedure and taking advantage of the results of
[
9
]
. We recall that given
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
and
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, Alberti’s Rank-One Theorem (see
[
2
, Corollary 4.6]
and
[
3
,
23
,
37
]
) ensures that for
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in A
the matrix
dD^{c}u/d|D^{c}u|(x)
has rank one, i.e., there exists two Borel functions
a_{u}\colon A\to{\mathbb{S}}^{k-1}
and
\nu_{u}\colon A\to{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
such that for
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in A
we have

(7.2)

\frac{dD^{c}u}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)=a_{u}(x)\otimes\nu_{u}(x).

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
,
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, and
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Assume that there exists a function
\hat{f}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
satisfying (
7.1
). For
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, for every
\lambda\geq 1
, and
\rho>0
we set

(7.3)

s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x):=\frac{|D^{c}u|(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\xi_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x):=s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)(a_{u}(x)\otimes\nu_{u}(x)),

where
(a_{u}(x),\nu_{u}(x))\in{\mathbb{S}}^{k-1}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
is given by (
7.2
).
Then

(7.4)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)=+\infty\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\rho s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)=0\,\,\,\ \text{for every $\lambda\geq 1$},

(7.5)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{dE^{c}(u,\cdot)}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)=\lim_{\lambda\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)},

for
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in A
.

Proof.

Let us fix
\delta>0
and consider the function
E_{\delta}
defined by (
5.6
). It is shown in
[
9
, Lemma 3.7]
that (
7.4
) holds true for
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in A
and that for
\delta>0
one has

\frac{dE^{c}(u,\cdot)}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)+\delta=\frac{dE^{c}_{\delta}(u,\cdot)}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)=\lim_{\lambda\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)}.

Since
E\leq E_{\delta}
, we deduce that

\frac{dE^{c}(u,\cdot)}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)+\delta\geq\limsup_{\lambda\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)}.

Thus, it is enough to show that there exists a constant
K>0
, depending only on
c_{1},...,c_{4}
and on
k
, such that for every
\lambda\geq 1
, for every
\delta>0
, and for
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in A
we have

(7.6)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)}\leq\Big(1+K\delta\Big)\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)}.

We fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\lambda\geq 1
, and consider a point
x\in A
such that (
7.2
) and (
7.4
) hold. By Theorem
5.16
hypothesis (
5.15
) of Corollary
5.10
is satisfied. Thus, there exists a function
u_{\rho}^{x}\in BV(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}u^{x}_{\rho}=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}
and
\|u^{x}_{\rho}-\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho
, such that

(7.7)

E(u^{x}_{\rho},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))+\frac{C_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}}{m}\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d},

where
c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}
and
C_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}
are the positive constants defined by (
5.14
) and (
5.26
), respectively.
Recalling that
\|u^{x}_{\rho}-\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}\|\leq c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho
and observing that
|[u^{x}_{\rho}]|=|[u^{x}_{\rho}-\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}]|
, we deduce that
|[u^{x}_{\rho}]|\leq 2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho
. Therefore, we may estimate

\displaystyle\int_{J_{u_{\rho}^{x}}}|[u^{x}_{\rho}]|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(J_{u^{x}_{\rho}}\setminus J_{u_{\rho}^{x}}^{1})}|[u^{x}_{\rho}]|\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}(J_{u_{\rho}^{x}}^{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho)\int_{J_{u_{\rho}^{x}}}|[u^{x}_{\rho}]|\land 1\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}.

From this inequality, by (c1) and (c2) of Definition
3.8
we see that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826ptE_{\delta}(u^{x}_{\rho},Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho))=E(u^{x}_{\rho},Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho))+\delta|D^{c}u^{x}_{\rho}|(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\leq E(u^{x}_{\rho},Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho))+\delta\int_{Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho)}|\nabla u^{x}_{\rho}|\,dx+\delta|D^{c}u|(Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\hskip-82.51282pt\displaystyle+\delta(1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho)\int_{J_{u_{\rho}^{x}}}|[u^{x}_{\rho}]|\land 1\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\Big(1+\delta\frac{1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho}{c_{1}}\Big)E(u^{x}_{\rho},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))+\delta\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}(1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho)\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}.

Since
\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}u^{x}_{\rho}=\text{tr}_{Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}^{\lambda}(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}
, combining the previous inequality with (
7.7
),
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\displaystyle\frac{m^{E_{\delta}}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}^{\lambda}(x)}\leq\Big(1+\delta\frac{1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho}{c_{1}}\Big)\Big(\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}+\frac{C_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)m}\Big)

(7.8)

\displaystyle\hphantom{m^{E_\delta}(\ell_{\xi^\lambda_\rho(x)},Q^\lambda_{\nu_u(x)}(x,\rho))}\displaystyle+\delta\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}(1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho)\frac{1}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}.

It is immediate to check that when
s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)\geq 1
we have

\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}\leq c_{3}k^{1/2}+c_{4}.

Hence, from this inequality, (
5.14
), (
5.26
), and (
7.4
), we see that there exist two cosntants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depending only on the structural constants
