c_{1},...,c_{4}
,
k
, and
d
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\delta(1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho)}{c_{1}}\Big(\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}+\frac{C_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)m}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\delta}{c_{1}}\Big(1+2(\sigma^{m}+1)\lambda\rho\big(s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)d^{1/2}+C_{1}\big)\Big)\Big((c_{3}k^{1/2}+c_{4})+\frac{C_{\xi^{\lambda_{\rho}(x)}}}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)m}\Big)

(7.9)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{c_{1}}\Big(c_{3}k^{1/2}+c_{4}+\frac{C_{2}}{m}\Big),

As for the remaining terms of (
7.8
), by (
5.26
) we see that

(7.10)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{C_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)m}=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{2c_{3}Cs^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)+2C(c_{4}+1)}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)m}=\frac{2c_{3}C}{m},

while by (
5.14
) and (
7.4
) we get that

(7.11)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}\delta\big(1+2c_{\xi^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x),m}\lambda\rho\big)\frac{1}{s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}=0.

Finally, using (
7.9
)-(
7.11
) we can take the limsup in (
7.8
) as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, obtaining an estimate that depends on
m
and
\delta
. Then, taking the limit as
m\to+\infty
we get (
7.6
), concluding the proof.
∎

The next result ensures that estimates on the volume integrand
f
translate into estimates on the right-hand side of (
7.5
). For the proof of this result we refer the reader to
[
20
, Lemma 5.3]
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\lambda\geq 1
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
t>0
, and
\mu\in[0,+\infty)
. Assume that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and
\rho>0
we have

m^{E}_{t\rho}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))\leq\mu\rho^{d}.

Then

m^{E}_{t\lambda\rho}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\mu\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}

for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and
\rho>0
. If, in addition, there exists some
x_{0}\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
such that

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}_{t\rho}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x_{0},\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\mu,

then

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}_{t\lambda\rho}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x_{0},\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}=\mu.

With these two lemmas at hand, we are now ready to prove Theorem
7.1
.

Proof of Theorem
7.1
.

Thanks to Theorem
5.16
and to (
7.1
), for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
we have

(7.12)

\displaystyle E^{a}(u,B)=\int_{B}\hat{f}(\nabla u)\,dx\quad\text{ and }\quad E^{s}(u,B)=\int_{J_{u}\cap B}g(x,[u],\nu_{u})\,d{\mathcal{H}}^{d-1}

for every
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

We are left with proving that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
we can represent the Cantor part
E^{c}
as

(7.13)

E^{c}(u,B)=\int_{B}\hat{f}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{dD^{c}u}{d|D^{c}u|}\Big)\,d|D^{c}u|

for every
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

Since
\ell_{\xi}
is an admissible function for the minimisation problem
m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))
, by (
7.12
) we get that

m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))\leq\hat{f}(\xi)\rho^{d}

for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},

\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, and
\rho>0
.
Recalling (
5.9
) and (
7.1
), applying Lemma
7.3
we obtain

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho)))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}}=\hat{f}(\xi)

for every
\lambda\geq 1
.
In particular,
for every
t>0
we have

(7.14)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t}=\frac{\hat{f}(t\xi)}{t}

so that, taking the
\limsup
for
t\to+\infty
, we get

(7.15)

\limsup_{t\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(t\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t}=\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi).

We claim that for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\lambda\geq 1
, and
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
we have

(7.16)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}=\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi)

whenever
s_{\rho}\to+\infty
and
\rho s_{\rho}\to 0^{+}
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
. To see this, first note that by (
5.9
) and (
7.1
) for every
\rho>0
we have

m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\hat{f}(s_{\rho}\xi)\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}
, whence

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}\leq\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi).

Thus, to prove (
7.16
) it is enough to show that

(7.17)

\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi)\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}.

Let
\eta>0
. By definition of
\hat{f}^{\infty}
, there exists
t_{\eta}>1/\eta
such that

(7.18)

\displaystyle\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi)-\eta<\frac{\hat{f}(t_{\eta}\xi)}{t_{\eta}}.

Hence, recalling (
7.14
) we have

(7.19)

\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi)-\eta\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t_{\eta}}.

Let us fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. By Lemma
6.11
we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho t_{\eta}}\leq\limsup_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}

\displaystyle+\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\vartheta_{m}(s_{\rho}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho)\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}+\frac{c_{7}}{s_{\rho}^{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}\Big)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{s_{\rho}}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{m}(s_{\rho}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho))\frac{M_{\xi}}{m}+\Big(\frac{1}{s_{\rho}^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{s_{\rho}}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\vartheta_{m}(t_{\eta}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho)\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t_{\eta}}+\frac{c_{7}}{t_{\eta}^{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t_{\eta}}\Big)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{t_{\eta}}

(7.20)

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\vartheta_{m}(t_{\eta}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho))\frac{M_{\xi}}{m}+\Big(\frac{1}{t_{\eta}^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t_{\eta}}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\Big),

where
M_{\xi}>0
is the constant of Lemma
6.11
, which we recall is independent of
\rho,s_{\rho}
,
t
, and
m
.

We separately study the summands of this last expression. Since
\vartheta_{m}
is continuous and
\vartheta_{m}(0)=0
, while
s_{\rho}\rho\to 0
and
s_{\rho}\to+\infty
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, recalling that
\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
\rho
by (c2) of Definition
3.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\vartheta_{m}(s_{\rho}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho))\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}+\frac{c_{7}}{s_{\rho}^{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}\Big)^{1-\alpha}

(7.21)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{c_{7}}{s_{\rho}}+(1+\vartheta_{m}(s_{\rho}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho))\frac{M_{\xi}}{m}+\Big(\frac{1}{s_{\rho}^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{s_{\rho}}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\Big)=\frac{M_{\xi}}{m}.

By (c2) of Definition
3.8
there exists
N_{\xi}>0
, independent of
\rho
and
t_{\eta}
, such that

\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t_{\eta}}\leq N_{\xi}.

Therefore, arguing as in the proof of (
7.21
), by (
7.18
) and (
7.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\vartheta_{m}(t_{\eta}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho)\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t_{\eta}}+\frac{c_{7}}{t_{\eta}^{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{m^{E}(t_{\eta}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}t_{\eta}}\Big)^{1-\alpha}+\frac{c_{7}}{t_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+(1+\vartheta_{m}(t_{\eta}\kappa_{\xi,m}\lambda\rho))\frac{M_{\xi}}{m}+\Big(\frac{1}{t_{\eta}^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t_{\eta}}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\Big)

(7.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{N_{\xi}^{1-\alpha}}{t_{\eta}^{\alpha}}+\frac{c_{7}}{t_{\eta}}+\Big(\frac{1}{t_{\eta}^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{t_{\eta}}\Big)\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}<N_{\xi}^{1-\alpha}\eta^{\alpha}+c_{7}\eta+(\eta+\eta^{\alpha})\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}},

where in the last inequality we have used that
t_{\eta}>1/\eta
.
Finally, combining (
7.19
) and (
7.22
), we get

\hat{f}^{\infty}(\xi)-\eta-\frac{M_{\xi}}{m}-N_{\xi}^{1-\alpha}\eta^{\alpha}+c_{7}\eta+(\eta+\eta^{\alpha})\frac{M_{\xi}}{m^{1-\alpha}}\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(s_{\rho}\ell_{\xi},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s_{\rho}}.

Letting
m\to+\infty
and
\eta\to 0^{+}
, we obtain (
7.17
).

Consider now a function
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Let
a_{u}(x)
,
\nu_{u}(x)
, and
s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)
be as in (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), and set
\xi(x):=a_{u}(x)\otimes\nu_{u}(x)
. By Lemma
7.2
for
|D^{c}u|
-a.e.
x\in A
we have

(7.23)

\frac{dE^{c}(u,\cdot)}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)=\lim_{\lambda\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)\ell_{\xi(x)},Q^{\lambda}_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\lambda^{d-1}\rho^{d}s^{\lambda}_{\rho}(x)}.

Recalling (
7.4
), in light of (
7.16
) and of (
7.23
), we then infer that

\frac{dE^{c}(u,\cdot)}{d|D^{c}u|}(x)=\hat{f}^{\infty}(a_{u}(x)\otimes\nu_{u}(x)),

which by (
7.2
) gives (
7.13
) for every
u\in BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
.

Let us assume now that
u\in GBV_{\star}(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and for every
R>0
we consider the set
A^{R}_{u}
introduced in Proposition
2.5
. We claim that it is enough to prove (
7.13
) for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
for which there exists
R>0
such that
B\subset A^{R}_{u}
. Indeed, by Proposition
2.4
we have
A_{u,0}^{R}\nearrow A_{\rm reg}:=\{x\in A\colon\widetilde{u}(x)\,\,\text{exists}\}
and that
|D^{c}u|(A\setminus A_{\rm reg})=0
. Hence, every
B\in\mathcal{B}(A)
can be written, up to a
|D^{c}u|
-negligble set, as the increasing union of Borel sets each contained in
A^{R}_{u,0}
, for some
R
. This proves the claim.

Let us fix
R>0
and a Borel set
B\subset A^{R}_{u}
. Let
R_{m}>R
be a sequence with
R_{m}\to+\infty
. Thanks to Lemma
5.2
, we have

(7.24)

\lim_{m\to+\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}E^{c}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)=E^{c}(u,B).

Since each function
\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u
belongs to
BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})

and the integral representation holds in
BV(A;{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, we have

(7.25)

E^{c}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u,B)=\int_{B}\hat{f}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{dD^{c}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u)}{d|D^{c}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u)|}\Big)\,d|D^{c}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u)|

for every
i\in\{1,...,m\}
. Recalling that
B\subset A^{R}_{u}\subset A^{R_{m}}_{u}
, from Proposition
2.5
and from (
3.4
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{dD^{c}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u)}{d|D^{c}(\psi^{i}_{R_{m}}\circ u)|}=\frac{dD^{c}u}{d|D^{c}u|}\quad|D^{c}u|\text{-a.e. in }A^{R}_{u},

\displaystyle\displaystyle|D^{c}(\psi_{R_{m}}^{i}\circ u)|=|D^{c}u|\quad\text{as measures in $A^{R}_{u}$},

for every
i\in\{1,...,m\}
. These equalities, together with (
7.24
) and (
7.25
), give (
7.13
), concluding the proof. ∎

8.
Integrands of the
\Gamma
-limits

In this section we consider a sequence
(E_{n})_{n}
of functionals in
\mathfrak{E}
. In the first part we assume that
(E_{n})_{n}

\Gamma
-converges to some functional
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
and we characterise the bulk and surface integrands
f
and
g
of
E
at a point
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
by taking first the limit as
n\to+\infty
of the infima of suitable minimisation problems for
E_{n}
on small cubes and taking then the limit as these cubes shrink to
x
.

In the last part we assume that each functional
E_{n}
belongs to
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
and prove the converse of the previous results: if the limits mentioned above exist and are independent of
x
, then they define two integrands
f
and
g
such that the sequence
(E_{n})_{n}

\Gamma
-converges to the functional corresponding to
f
and
g
.

We begin this analysis by showing that it is possible to obtain the function
f
introduced in (
5.9
) by means of limits of constrained minimisation problems.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
and let
f
be the function defined by (
5.9
). Then there exists a
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-negligible set
N\in\mathcal{B}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, such that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
and for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
we have

(8.1)

f(x,\xi)=\lim_{m\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}_{\rho c_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}},

where
c_{\xi,m}
is the constant defined by (
5.14
) and
m^{E}_{t}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))
is given by (
5.8
) with
t=c_{\xi,m}\rho
.
If, in addition, there exists a function
\hat{f}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
such that
f(x,\xi)=\hat{f}(\xi)
for
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, then (
8.1
) holds for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
.

Proof.

It follows immediately from (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) that for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
we have

m^{E}\leq m^{E}_{c_{\xi,m}\rho}
. Thus, we only have to prove that

(8.2)

\lim_{m\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}_{\rho c_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}

for
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
.
Thanks to Theorem
5.16
, the equality (
5.15
) is satisfied. Hence, by
Corollary
5.10
there exists a set
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(N)=0
, satisfying the following property: for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\rho>0
small enough there exists a function
u\in BV(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
such that with tr
{}_{Q(x,\rho)}u=
tr
{}_{Q(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
,
\|u-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq c_{\xi,m}\rho
, and such that

m^{E}_{\rho c_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+\frac{C_{\xi}}{m}\rho^{d},

where
C_{\xi}>0
is the constant defined by (
5.26
).
Dividing this inequality by
\rho^{d}
and letting
\rho\to 0^{+}
, we conclude that

\limsup_{\rho\to^{0}+}\frac{m^{E}_{\rho c_{\xi,m}}(u,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{m^{E}(u,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}+\frac{C_{\xi}}{m}.

Taking the limit for
m\to+\infty
, we obtain (
8.2
).

To conclude the proof, we note that under the additional hypothesis we have
N=\mathchar 31\relax
in Corollary
5.10
.
∎

The next result is useful to understand the relation between the minima of problems associated with a sequence
(E_{n})_{n}
on an open set
A^{\prime}
and the minimisation of the problem associated with their
\Gamma
-limit
E
, computed on a larger open set
A^{\prime\prime}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}
,
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
, and let
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
with Lipschitz boundary and such that
A^{\prime}\subset\subset A^{\prime\prime}
. Assume that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E(\cdot,A)
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Then for every
w\in W^{1,1}_{\rm loc}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, we have

m^{E}(w,A^{\prime\prime})\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}m^{E_{n}}(w,A^{\prime})+c_{3}k^{1/2}\int_{A\setminus A^{\prime}}|\nabla w|\,dx+c_{4}{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(A^{\prime\prime}\setminus A^{\prime}).

Proof.

The proof can be obtained by adapting the arguments of
[
20
, Proposition 3.1]
, replacing
[
21
, Theorem 7.13]
by
[
24
, Theorem 3.22]
.
∎

We now prove a result that allows to compare the limit of the minima of problems associated with a sequence
(E_{n})_{n}
on a cube
Q(x,\rho)
with the minimum of the problem associated with the
\Gamma
-limit
E
, computed on the same cube.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}
,
E\in\mathfrak{E}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\rho>0
, and
s>d^{1/2}|\xi|\rho
. Assume that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E(\cdot,A)
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Then

(8.3)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}m_{t}^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))\leq m_{s}^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+\frac{K_{\xi}}{m}\rho^{d}

where
t:=2\sigma^{m}(s+d^{1/2}|\xi|\rho)+d^{1/2}|\xi|\rho
and
K_{\xi}>0
is a constant depending on
\xi
, but not on
(f_{n})_{n}
,
(g_{n})_{n}
,
m
,
s
, and
\rho
.

Proof.

Let us fix
0<\eta<1
. Consider a function
u\in BV(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\|u-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq s
and
\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}u=\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
, such that

(8.4)

E(u,Q(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}_{s}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+\eta\leq(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})\rho^{d}+\eta.

By Lemma
6.8
there exists a sequence of functions
(v_{n})_{n}\subset BV(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho))}\leq 2\sigma^{m}(s+d^{1/2}|\xi|\rho)
, such that
v_{n}\to u
in
L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})

(8.5)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(v_{n},Q(x,\rho))\leq E(u,Q(x,\rho))+C\frac{E(u,Q(x,\rho))+\rho^{d}}{m}.

Let us fix
0<\eta<1
and
0<r<\rho
. For every
0<\delta\leq\eta
we apply Lemma
4.2
to the open sets
A=Q(x,r)
and
U=Q(x,\rho)\setminus\overline{Q(x,r)}
to obtain a sequence
(u_{n})_{n}\subset BV(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
converging to
u
in
L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, with
\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}u_{n}=\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
and
\|u_{n}-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq 2\sigma^{m}(s+d^{1/2}|\xi|\rho)+d^{1/2}|\xi|\rho=t
, such that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(u_{n},Q(x,\rho))\leq(1+\delta)\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big(E_{n}(v_{n},Q(x,\rho))+E_{n}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho)\setminus\overline{Q(x,r)})\Big)+\eta

Exploiting (
8.4
) and (
8.5
), from this last inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}E_{n}(u_{n},Q(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}_{s}(u,Q(x,\rho))+\eta+\delta\big((c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})\rho^{d}+\eta\big)

(8.6)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad\quad\qquad+(1+\delta)C\frac{(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4}+1)\rho^{d}+\eta}{m}+(1+\delta)(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(\rho^{d}-r^{d})+\eta.

Choosing
r
so that
2(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(\rho^{d}-r^{d})\leq\eta
and
\delta
such that
\delta((c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})\rho^{d}+\eta)\leq\eta
, recalling that
\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}u_{n}=\text{tr}_{Q(x,\rho)}\ell_{\xi}
and
\|u_{n}-\ell_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho);{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})}\leq t
, from (
8.6
) we obtain

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}m^{E_{n}}_{t}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))\leq m^{E}_{s}(u,Q(x,\rho))+2C\frac{(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4}+1)\rho^{d}+\eta}{m}+4\eta.

We conclude the proof by letting
\eta\to 0^{+}
.
∎

The next result shows that when
E
is the
\Gamma
-limit of a sequence of functionals
(E_{n})_{n}
, the value of its bulk integrand
f
at
(x,\xi)
can be obtained by taking first the limit of
m^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))/\rho^{d}
as
n\to+\infty
and then the limit as
\rho\to 0^{+}
. For technical reasons, we need also a similar result where we replace
m^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))
by its constrained version
m^{E_{n}}_{t}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))
, for a suitable choice of
t>0
.

Given
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, the constraint
t
will be given by
\rho\beta_{\xi,m}
, with

(8.7)

\beta_{\xi,m}:=\sigma^{m}(c_{\xi,m}+d^{1/2}|\xi|)+d^{1/2}|\xi|,

where
c_{\xi,m}>0
is the constant defined by (
5.14
).

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}
,
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
, and let
f
be the function defined by (
5.9
). Assume that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E(\cdot,A)
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
. Then there exist an
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-negligible set
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
such that for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
we have

(8.8)

\displaystyle\displaystyle f(x,\xi)=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}},

(8.9)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322ptf(x,\xi)=\!\!\!\!\!\lim_{m\to+\infty}\!\!\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\!\!\!\!\!\lim_{m\to+\infty}\!\!\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}},

where
\beta_{\xi,m}
is defined by (
8.7
).

If in addition there exists a function
\hat{f}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
such that
f(x,\xi)=\hat{f}(\xi)
for
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, then (
8.8
) and (
8.9
) hold for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
.

Proof.

Let
N\in\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
be the union of the
{\mathcal{L}}^{d}
-negligible sets of Corollary
5.10
and Lemma
8.1
. We fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
\rho>0
, and set
r:=\rho+\rho^{2}
. Using first Lemma
8.2
, then Lemma
8.3
, and finally Corollary
5.10
, for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\setminus N
and
\rho>0
small enough we have

\displaystyle m^{E}(\ell_{\xi};Q(x,r))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to+\infty}m^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(r^{d}-\rho^{d})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to+\infty}m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(r^{d}-\rho^{d})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(r^{d}-\rho^{d})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m^{E}_{\rho c_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(r^{d}-\rho^{d})+\frac{K_{\xi}}{m}\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m^{E}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(r^{d}-\rho^{d})+\frac{K_{\xi}+C_{\xi}}{m}\rho^{d},

where
K_{\xi}
is the constant of Lemma
8.3
and
C_{\xi}
is given by (
5.26
).
We divide all terms of the previous chain of inequalities by
\rho^{d}
, and take first the limsup for
\rho\to 0^{+}
and then the limit for
m\to+\infty
. By (
5.9
) in this way we obtain (
8.9
), since
(r^{d}-\rho^{d})/\rho^{d}\to 0
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
.

To prove (
8.8
), one can simply replace the expression in the second line of the previous chain of inequalities by

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}m^{E_{n}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))+(c_{3}k^{1/2}|\xi|+c_{4})(r^{d}-\rho^{d}).

If the additional hypothesis is satisfied, the last lines of Corollary
5.10
and of Lemma
8.8
ensure that
N=\mathchar 31\relax
, which concludes the proof.
∎

In the next proposition we show that an equality similar to (
8.8
) holds also for the surface integrand
g
.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}
, let
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
, and let
g
be given by (
5.11
). Assume that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E(\cdot,A)
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
. Then for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have

(8.10)

g(x,\zeta,\nu)=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}.

Proof.

The proof can be obtained with the same arguments of
[
20
, Proposition 3.3]
.
∎

We conclude this section by stating a fundamental result for the proof of the homogenisation theorem, which will be the object of the next section. We show that for sequences of functions
(f_{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
and
(g_{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
, a sufficient conditions for
E^{f_{n},g_{n}}
to
\Gamma
-converge on every bounded open set is that (
8.9
) and (
8.10
) hold and that the function
f
is independent of
x
.

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
(E_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
. Assume that there exist
\hat{f}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
and
\hat{g}\colon{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip-22.76228pt\hat{f}(\xi)=\lim_{m\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\lim_{m\to+\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}},

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{g}(x,\zeta,\nu)=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}(u_{x,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}},

for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, where
\beta_{\xi,m}
is given by (
8.7
). Then
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
,
\hat{g}\in\mathcal{G}
and for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E^{\hat{f},\hat{g}}
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
, where
E^{\hat{f},\hat{g}}
is as in Definition
3.7
.

Proof.

The proof follows closely the lines of
[
20
, Theorem 5.4]
. By Theorem
4.1
there exists a subsequence, not relabelled, and a functional
E\in\mathfrak{E}
such that for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
and by Proposition
6.10

E\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
.
Thanks to Theorem
5.16
, the functions
f
and
g
defined by (
5.9
) and (
5.11
), respectively, satisfy (
5.40
) and (
5.41
), and
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}
. Additionally, by Proposition
8.4
and Proposition
8.5
,
f=\hat{f}
and
g=\hat{g}
, so that (
7.1
) holds. Hence, by Theorem
7.1
we have that
E=E^{\hat{f},\hat{g}}
. Since the functions
\hat{f}
and
\hat{g}
are independent of the chosen subsequence, for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
by the Urysohn property of
\Gamma
-convergence (see
[
17
, Proposition 8.3]
) the original sequence
E_{n}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E^{\hat{f},\hat{g}}(\cdot,A)
, concluding the proof.
∎

9.
Homogenisation of free-discontinuity functionals

We are now ready to deal with the homogenisation of functionals in
\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}
. The arguments that we will make use of are based on the method devised by in
[
20
, Section 6]
.
The main difference with respect to
[
20
]
, is the dependence on
m
of the constant
\beta_{\xi,m}
appearing in (
8.6
), which, in the problem of stochastic homogenisation, forces us to use the Subadditive Ergodic Theorem at
m
fixed and only then to pass to the limit for
m\to+\infty
.

Before introducing the stochastic setting, we state a result which shows that, in the case of homogenisation, the sufficient conditions for
\Gamma
-convergence presented in Theorem
8.6
can be rewritten in terms of cubes whose side length
r
tends to
+\infty
. This formulation will be more suitable for the stochastic setting.

Given
f\in\mathcal{F^{\alpha}}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
for every
\varepsilon>0
we set
f_{\varepsilon}(x,\xi):=f(x/\varepsilon,\xi)
and
g_{\varepsilon}(x,\zeta,\nu):=g(x/\varepsilon,\zeta,\nu)
for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. We observe that
f_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
and
g_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
. We set
E_{\varepsilon}:=E^{f_{\varepsilon},g_{\varepsilon}}
according to Definition
3.7
. We recall that
f^{\infty}
and
g^{0}
are the functions given by (
3.2
) and (
6.3
), that
f^{\infty}\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
, while
g^{0}\notin\mathcal{G}
, since it does not satisfy (g3).

The following theorem provides a general condition that guarantees the
\Gamma
-convergence of
E_{\varepsilon}
towards an integral functional whose integrands do not depend on
x
. We shall see in Remark
9.10
that the hypotheses are satisfied in the case where
f
and
g
are periodic with respect to
x
. The advantage of these formulation is that these hypotheses are satisfied almost surely under the standard hypotheses of stochastic homogenisation.

Theorem 9.1
.

Assume that there exists a function
g_{\rm hom}\colon{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
and that for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a function
f_{\rm hom}^{m}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
such that

(9.1)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055ptf_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi)=\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E^{f,g^{0}}}_{r\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(rx,r))}{r^{d}}\quad\text{ for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},\,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d},

(9.2)

\displaystyle\displaystyle g_{\rm hom}(\zeta,\nu)=\!\!\!\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E^{f^{\infty},g}}(u_{rx,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(rx,r))}{r^{d-1}}\quad\text{ for every }x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d},\,\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k},\text{ and }\nu\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}.

Let
f_{\rm hom}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
be the function defined by

(9.3)

f_{\rm hom}(\xi):=\lim_{m\to+\infty}f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi)=\inf_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi)

for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
. Then
f_{\rm hom}\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
,
g_{\rm hom}\in\mathcal{G}
,
E^{f_{\rm hom},g_{\rm hom}}\in\mathfrak{E}^{\alpha,\vartheta}_{w}
, and for every
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0^{+}
and for every
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
the sequence
E_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E^{f_{\rm hom},g_{\rm hom}}(\cdot,A)
with respect to the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.

Proof.

We first observe that the limit in (
9.3
) exists, since
m\mapsto m^{E^{f,g^{0}}}_{r\beta_{\xi,m}}
is non-increasing, hence, the same property holds for
m\mapsto f_{\rm hom}^{m}
.

Let us fix a sequence
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0^{+}
as
n\to+\infty
and set
E_{n}:=E^{f_{\varepsilon_{,}},g_{\varepsilon_{n}}}
. To prove the result, it is enough to show that the hypotheses of Theorem
8.6
are satisfied by
f_{\rm hom}
and
g_{\rm hom}
.
Since the hypothesis concerning
g_{\rm hom}
can checked by repeating verbatim the arguments of
[
20
, Theorem 6.3]
, we only prove that the hypothesis concerning
f_{\rm hom}
are satisfied.

To this aim, we fix
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\rho>0
and set
r_{n}:=\rho/\varepsilon_{n}
. The same computations performed in
[
20
, Lemma 6.1]
show that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
we have

(9.4)

\big|m^{E_{\varepsilon}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))-\varepsilon^{d}m_{(\rho/\varepsilon)\beta_{\xi,m}}^{E^{f,g^{0}}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x/\varepsilon,\rho/\varepsilon))\big|\leq K_{\xi}\vartheta(2\rho\beta_{\xi,m})\rho^{d},

where
K_{\xi}>0
is a constant depending on
\xi
, but not on
m
,\rho
, and
x
. Let us fix
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
. Using twice the previous inequality with
\varepsilon
replaced by
\varepsilon_{n}
, by (
9.1
) we get that

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptf_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi)\rho^{d}-K_{\xi}(2\beta_{\xi,m}\rho)\rho^{d}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}(\rho/r_{n})^{d}m_{r_{n}\beta_{\xi,m}}^{E^{f,g^{0}}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(r_{n}x/\rho,r_{n}))-K_{\xi}\vartheta(2\beta_{\xi,m}\rho)\rho^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}m_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}^{E_{\varepsilon_{n}}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))\leq\limsup_{n\to+\infty}m_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}^{E_{\varepsilon_{n}}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to+\infty}(\rho/r_{n})^{d}m_{r_{n}\beta_{\xi,m}}^{E^{f,g^{0}}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(r_{n}x/\rho,r_{n}))+K_{\xi}\vartheta(2\beta_{\xi,m}\rho)\rho^{d}=f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi)\rho^{d}+K_{\xi}\vartheta(2\beta_{\xi,m}\rho)\rho^{d}.

Since
\vartheta
is continuous and
\vartheta(0)=0
, dividing by
\rho^{d}
and taking the limsup for
\rho\to 0^{+}
we obtain

f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi)=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E_{n}}_{\rho\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}.

Taking the limit as
m\to+\infty
we obtain that the hypothesis for
f_{\rm hom}
in Theorem
8.6
is satisfied, so that the proof is concluded.
∎

The following result shows that hypothesis (
9.1
) can be slightly weakend

Lemma 9.2
.

Assume that for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, and
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}^{k\times d}
, the space of
k\times d
matrices with rational entries, the limit

f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\xi):=\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E^{f,g^{0}}}_{r\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(rx,r))}{r^{d}}

exists and is independent of
x
. Then the function
f_{\rm hom}^{m}
can be extended to a continuous function, still denoted by
f_{\rm hom}^{m}
, defined on the whole
{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
and such that (
9.1
) is satisfied.

Proof.

It is enough to repeat for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
the arguments of
[
20
, Lemma 6.4]
.
∎

We now introduce the stochastic setting in which we are going to deal with the homogenisation problem.

We fix a probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{T},P)
and a group
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
of
P
-preserving transformations on

(\Omega,\mathcal{T},P)
; that is, a family
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
of
\mathcal{T}
-measurable bijective maps
\tau_{z}\colon\Omega\to\Omega
such that

(a)

for every
E\in\mathcal{T}
and every
z\in\>{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
we have
P(\tau_{z}^{-1}(E))=P(E)
;

(b)

\tau_{0}=\text{id}
, the identity map on
\Omega
and for every
z,z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
one has
\tau_{z}\circ\tau_{z}^{\prime}=\tau_{z+z}
.

A group
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
of
P
-preserving transformations is said to be ergodic if for every set
E\in\mathcal{T}
with the property that
\tau_{z}(E)=E
for every
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
, has probability either
0
or
1
.
In analogy with
[
20
]
, we introduce two classes of stochastic integrands.

Definition 9.3
.

\mathcal{SF^{\alpha}}
is the collection of all
\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d})
-measurable functions
f\colon\Omega\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega
the function
f(\omega):=f(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to
\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
the following stochastic periodicity holds: for every
\omega\in\Omega
,
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
we have

f(\omega,x+z,\xi)=f(\tau_{z}(\omega),x,\xi).

\mathcal{SG^{\vartheta}}
is
the collection of all
\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
-measurable functions
g\colon\Omega\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega
the function
g(\omega):=g(\omega,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to
\mathcal{G}^{\vartheta}
, and the following stochastic periodicity holds:
for every
\omega\in\Omega
,
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have

g(\omega,x+z,\zeta,\nu)=g(\tau_{z}(\omega),x,\zeta,\nu).

We now give the definition of subadditive process.
Before doing this, we introduce
\mathcal{R}
the collection of rectangles defined by

\mathcal{R}:=\{R\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\colon R=[a_{1},b_{1})\times...\times[a_{d},b_{d}),\text{ for some }a,b\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}\text{ with }a_{i}<b_{i}\text{ for }i\in\{1,...,d\}\}.

We also introduce
(\Omega,\widehat{\mathcal{T}},\widehat{P})
the completion of
(\Omega,\mathcal{T},P)
. It is immediate to see that
(\tau_{z})_{z}
is a group of
P
-preserving transformation on
(\Omega,\widehat{\mathcal{T}},\widehat{P})
.

Definition 9.4
.

A function
\mu\colon\Omega\times\mathcal{R}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is said to be a covariant subadditive process with respect to
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
if the following properties are satisfied

(a)

for every
R\in\mathcal{R}
the function
\mu(\cdot,R)
is
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
-measurable;

(b)

for every
\omega\in\Omega
,
R\in\mathcal{R}
, and
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
we have
\mu(\omega,R+z)=\mu(\tau_{z}(\omega),R)
;

(c)

given
R\in\mathcal{R}
and a finite partition
(R_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\subset\mathcal{R}
of
R
, we have

\mu(\omega,R)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu(\omega,R_{i})

for every
\omega\in\Omega
;

(d)

there exists
C>0
such that
0\leq\mu(\omega,R)\leq C{\mathcal{L}}^{d}(R)
for every
\omega\in\Omega

and
R\in\mathcal{R}
.

We recall the Subadditive Ergodic Theorem of Ackoglu and Krengel
[
1
, Theorem 2.7]
. For the particular version here used we refer the reader to
[
18
, Proposition 1]
(see also
[
35
]
).

Theorem 9.5
.

Let
\mu
be a subadditive process with respect to the group
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
. Then there exist a
\mathcal{T}
-measurable set
\Omega^{\prime}
, with
P(\Omega^{\prime})=1
, and a function
\varphi\colon\Omega\to[0,+\infty)
such that

\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{\mu(\omega,Q(rx,r))}{r^{d}}=\varphi(\omega)

for every
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
and every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
. If the group
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
is also ergodic, then
\varphi
is constant
P
-a.e.

Let
f\in\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
and
g\in\mathcal{SG}^{\vartheta}
. For every
\omega\in\Omega
, we set
f^{\infty}(\omega):=f(\omega)^{\infty}
and
g^{0}(\omega):=g(\omega)^{0}
.

The following lemma shows that it is possible to define a subadditive process closely related to condition (
9.1
).

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
, let
g\in\mathcal{SG}^{\vartheta}
, let
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, and let
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For every
R\in\mathcal{R}
let
\rho(R)
be the length of the longest of its sides. Then the function
\Phi_{\xi,m}\colon\Omega\times\mathcal{R}\to[0,+\infty)
defined by

(9.5)

\Phi_{\xi,m}(\omega,R):=m^{E^{f(\omega),g^{0}(\omega)}}_{\rho(R)\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},R^{\circ})

is a covariant subadditive process.

Proof.

The proof can be obtained by arguing exactly as in
[
20
, Lemma 6.9]
.
∎

With this lemma at hand, we are ready to show that condition (
9.1
) with
f
replaced by
f(\omega)
and
g^{0}
replaced by
g^{0}(\omega)
is satisfied for
P
-a.e
\omega\in\Omega
and for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Proposition 9.7
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
and
g\in\mathcal{SG}^{\vartheta}
. Then there exist a
\mathcal{T}
-measurable set
\Omega^{\prime}
, with
P(\Omega^{\prime})=1
, such that for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a function
f_{\rm hom}^{m}\colon\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
, with
f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\cdot,\xi)

\mathcal{T}
-measurable for every
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, such that

\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E^{f(\omega),g^{0}(\omega)}}_{r\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(rx,r))}{r^{d}}=f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\omega,\xi)

for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
. Moreover, the function
f_{\rm hom}\colon\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
defined by

(9.6)

f_{\rm hom}(\omega,\xi):=\lim_{m\to+\infty}f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\omega,\xi)=\inf_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\omega,\xi)

belongs to
\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
.
If, in addition,
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
is ergodic, by choosing
\Omega^{\prime}
appropriately, we have that
f_{\rm hom}^{m}
and
f_{\rm hom}
are
independent of
\omega
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.6
for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and for every
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}^{k\times d}
the function
\Phi_{\xi,m}
defined by (
9.5
) is a covariant subadditive process. Hence, by the Subadditive Ergodic Theorem
9.5
, there exists a
\mathcal{T}
-measurable set
\Omega^{\prime}
, with
P(\Omega^{\prime})=1
, and for every
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
a function
f_{\rm hom}^{m}\colon\Omega\times\mathbb{Q}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
, with
f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\cdot,\xi)

\mathcal{T}
-measurable for every
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}^{k\times d}
, such that

(9.7)

\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E^{f(\omega),f^{0}(\omega)}}_{r\beta_{\xi,m}}(\ell_{\xi},Q(rx,r))}{r^{d}}=f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\omega,\xi)

for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, and
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}^{k\times d}
. Thanks to Lemma
9.2
, the function
f_{\rm hom}^{m}
can be extended to a function
f_{\rm hom}^{m}\colon\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
,
\mathcal{T}
-measurable with respect to
\omega
and continuous with respect to
\xi
, such that (
9.7
) holds for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
, and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
. We now fix
\omega_{0}\in\Omega
and we redefine
f_{\rm hom}^{m}
on
\Omega\setminus\Omega^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, by setting
f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\omega,\xi)=f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\omega_{0},\xi)
for every
\omega\in\Omega
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
. Note that for every
\omega\in\Omega
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}
, the sequence
f^{m}(\omega,\xi)
is non-increasing with respect to
m
, which justifies (
9.6
) and that
f_{\rm hom}(\cdot,\xi)
is
\mathcal{T}
-measurable. By Theorem
9.1
for every
\omega\in\Omega
the function
f_{\rm hom}(\omega)\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
, hence,
f_{\rm hom}\in\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
.

If, in addition,
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
is ergodic, then
f_{\rm hom}^{m}(\cdot,\xi)
is constant
P
-a.e. for every
m
. This leads to the last sentence of the statement, concluding the proof.
∎

The following proposition shows that
P
-a.e. in
\Omega
condition (
9.2
) is also satisfied.

Proposition 9.8
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
, let
g\in\mathcal{SG}^{\vartheta}
. Then there exists a
\mathcal{T}
-measurable set
\Omega^{\prime}
, with
P(\Omega^{\prime})=1
,
and a
\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{B}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
-measurable function
\hat{g}\colon\Omega\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
such that

(9.8)

\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{m^{E^{f^{\infty}(\omega),g(\omega)}}(u_{rx,\zeta,\nu},Q_{\nu}(rx,r))}{r^{d-1}}=\hat{g}(\omega,\zeta,\nu)

for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
,
\zeta\in{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. If, in addition, the group
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
is ergodic, by choosing
\Omega^{\prime}
appropriately, we have that
the function
g
is independent of
\omega
.

Proof.

The result is proved by adapting the same arguments used in
[
15
, Proposition 9.3, Proposition 9.4, Proposition 9.5]
.
∎

Combining Theorem
8.6
with Propositions
9.7
and Proposition
9.8
, we are finally able to obtain the desired stochastic homogenisation theorem.

Theorem 9.9
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{SF}^{\alpha}
, let
g\in\mathcal{SG}^{\vartheta}
, and for every
\varepsilon>0
and
\omega\in\Omega
let
E^{\omega}_{\varepsilon}:=E^{f_{\varepsilon}(\omega),g_{\varepsilon}(\omega)}
, according to Definition
3.7
. Let
f_{\rm hom}\colon\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
and
g_{\rm hom}\colon\Omega\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
be the functions given in Propositions
9.7
and
9.8
. Then for the function
f_{\rm hom}(\omega,\cdot)\in\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}
and
g_{\rm hom}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)\in\mathcal{G}
for every
\omega\in\Omega
and there exists
\mathcal{T}
-measurable set
\Omega^{\prime}
, with
P(\Omega^{\prime})=1
, such that
for every sequence
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0^{+}
,
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, and
A\in\mathcal{A}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d})
, the sequence
E^{\omega}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\cdot,A)

\Gamma
-converges to
E^{f_{\rm hom}(\omega),g_{\rm hom}(\omega)}(\cdot,A)
in the topology of
L^{0}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{d};{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k})
.

If, in addition, the group
(\tau_{z})_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
is ergodic, by choosing
\Omega^{\prime}
appropriately, the functions
f_{\rm hom}
and
g_{\rm hom}
are independent of
\omega
.

Remark 9.10
.

The periodic homogenisation in the deterministic case follows immediately from Theorem
9.9
once we note that in the case where
\Omega
consists of a single point and
\tau_{z}=Id
for every
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
, the stochastic periodicity reduces to the 1-periodicity in each variable.
